# Cele 697 de capabilități Hermes — status cod

[Statusul sprintului](../HERMES_STATUS.md) · [Reguli și livrări](HERMES_SPRINT.md)

ID-urile H001–H697 sunt poziții în inventarul înghețat, validate prin hash. Folosește căutarea paginii pentru nume/ID sau comanda `show` pentru cerința originală completă. «Audit 07.09» înseamnă moștenit, nu verificat din nou. Dovezile legate sunt fișiere inspectate; simpla lor existență nu dovedește întregul flux și nu afirmă că testele au fost rulate în această livrare.

| <a id="h002"></a>H002 | Run one scripted agent turn from a pipe and get a machine-readable cost report | copy | Parțial | Reevaluat | Two parts of the row are open. (1) THE SPEND HALF IS A SCHEMA OF NULLS. The hub cannot attribute one turn's cost. \`AgentConfig.model\` defaults to \`google/gemma-4-31b-a4b\` and none of the 18 agents in \`agents/_system/agents.yaml\` declares a model, so \`cost_tracker\` meters every turn at the default price row and reports $0.00; an id it does not know is priced from the \`default\` row with no \`priced\` flag. \`estimated_cost_usd\`, the token counts, \`api_calls\`, \`model\` and \`provider\` are therefore \`null\` with \`cost_basis: "unavailable"\` rather than measured zeros, and a test pins that they never become numbers. (2) A STOP THAT QUEUES NOTHING IS STILL JUDGED BY TEXT, AND ON A LIVE HUB THAT TEXT IS USUALLY REWRITTEN. The hub reports ids only for approvals queued through the gated Tool-RPC path. The tool loop's own stops are recognised only by containment over the reply. A turn routed to a specialist is not handed back verbatim: the orchestrator sends it through synthesis (\`was_synthesized = len(responses) \> 1 or "jarvis" not in responses\`). With a \`jarvis\` agent and a router loaded, \`jarvis.synthesize\` rewrites it through a model; only the fallbacks (no \`jarvis\` agent, no router, a failed synthesis call) produce the \`\[athena\]: …\` join that containment survives. So a specialist's tool-loop stop can come back reworded and exit 0. The table also has no entry for \`THINKING_EXHAUSTED_REPLY\` (agents.core.llm.base): a comment in the table names it, but no row follows. A reasoning model that spent its whole budget thinking is therefore printed as the answer with exit 0. Also open: \`POST /chat\` returns no trace, invocation or session id, so \`session_id\` is echoed only when the caller supplied one; \`-z\` does not silence hub-side logging; there is no \`--output-format\` beyond the existing \`--json\`; a piped body is read whole up to the bound rather than streamed; and \`os.replace\` onto a symlinked \`--usage-file\` path replaces the link with a regular file instead of writing through it. [d1](../agents/cli/nerva.py) [d2](../tests/test_nerva_oneshot.py) [d3](../tests/test_nerva_cli.py) |

| <a id="h042"></a>H042 | Start, check and stop the web UI / headless backend | update | Parțial | Reevaluat | Hermes's row is start, check \*\*and stop\*\*, and the stop half is entirely absent: no \`stop\` verb, no pidfile, no ready file. The row cites \`hermes dashboard --status/--stop\` plus backend ready sentinels and a ready file (06-cli-f.md, 16 entries); Nerva has \`nerva status\`, which reads the HTTP surface and so cannot report a hub that is starting or wedged before the port opens, and Ctrl-C. Hermes also signals the conflict as a machine-readable \`BACKEND_PORT_IN_USE\`, where this slice signals it as an exit code and an English sentence — enough for a supervisor, not enough for a caller parsing a status stream. Not delivered either: \`--isolated\`, \`--skip-build\`, \`--no-open\`, the SSH session-token file and owner-nonce, or the interactive auth-setup prompt. \*\*One known limit, in the safe direction:\*\* the probe picks a single address family from the host string while uvicorn binds every address \`getaddrinfo\` returns, so a dual-stack name like \`localhost\` is probed on v4 only and can miss a conflict held on the v6 address — it misses silently, and uvicorn then reports it exactly as it does today. The Windows half of the \`SO_REUSEADDR\` choice is read off asyncio's \`reuse_address = os.name == "posix"\` line and has never been executed here; that test skips off POSIX and the docstring says so. [d1](../serve.py) [d2](../tests/test_h2311_operability.py) [d3](../docs/test-manual/01-environment-and-boot.md) |

Memoria nu e costată: e per sesiune și variabilă, nu cost fix, iar a o raporta ca „fix" ar fi exact tipul de cifră greșită pe care livrarea asta o evită. Schemele de unelte sunt raportate doar când i se dau: verbul nu bootează un registru, iar pe o cutie fără hub secțiunea lipsește și raportul \*\*spune\*\* că lipsește, în loc s-o raporteze ca zero. Cost per toolset (grupat) nu e implementat, doar per unealtă. Neverificat pe serviciu real: nicio cerere nu a fost trimisă — cifrele sunt estimări comparabile cu bugetul intern, nu contorul unui provider. | <a id="h060"></a>H060 | Talking to a busy agent: queue / steer / interrupt, \`/stop\`, and the ack that says what happened | copy | Parțial | Reevaluat | No busy-input semantics. A mid-turn message cannot redirect or interrupt the running turn. There is no explicit FIFO \`/queue\`, no \`/steer\` into the running turn and no configurable mode between these with downgrades. \`/stop\` is not a hard kill: it engages the e-stop but does not cancel the running turn or unlock the session. It is also dispatched inside \`handle_input\` (agents/core/orchestrator.py:1739), so it waits behind the same lease. While a turn holds the lease past the wait bound, \`/stop\` itself is answered with \`TURN_BUSY_REPLY\` (named at agents/core/orchestrator.py:2880). There is one busy string, not a set of acks saying whether a message was queued, steered or interrupted. None of the protective demotions exist (running subagent, in-flight compression, photo burst, very fast follow-up merged as the same intent). The lease is the only slot reservation; there is no pending-agent sentinel. \`SlashCommand\` (agents/core/commands.py:61) carries no \`busy_policy\`. [d1](../agents/core/orchestrator.py) [d2](../agents/core/commands.py) [d3](../agents/web.py) |
| <a id="h061"></a>H061 | Turn serialization and per-session runtime state (lease, run generations, slot claim, concurrency cap, stale/reaped eviction, warm agent cache) | copy | Parțial | Reevaluat | No run generation lets a late callback from an abandoned turn detect that it no longer owns the session. The only compare-and-set is the checkpoint-clock commit of a compaction (agents/core/orchestrator.py:3160), and it guards a summary, not turn ownership. There is no active-session slot claimed before the first await and no \`max_concurrent_sessions\` cap: turns on different sessions run concurrently without bound (tests/test_turn_lease.py:88). No watchdog evicts a session whose turn is wedged: its lease stays held until the turn ends, and later messages only get the busy reply. There is no eviction of an in-memory slot whose durable routing row ended, and no single per-session \`SessionState\` object with explicit lifetimes. There is no per-session warm agent cache and no memory-pressure eviction. Agents are process-wide: every session shares \`orchestrator.agents\`, which the compaction-boundary persona refresh walks as a whole (agents/core/orchestrator.py:350). | <a id="h063"></a>H063 | Session reset policy and expiry/stall watchers | copy | Parțial | Reevaluat | 1. Reset policy: idle-minutes and daily-at-hour resets, overridable per session type (dm/group/thread) and per channel, applied by rotating the channel session to a fresh id while keeping the old transcript. 2. Reset triggers: configurable texts (e.g. /new, /reset) intercepted in channel_handler before a turn runs; they start a fresh session and acknowledge it. 3. Expiry watcher: a scheduled job that applies the idle/daily policies and drops stale _channel_sessions entries. 4. Stall watcher: track inbound receipt against last turn progress per session and notify the owner once when a message has been pending past a threshold with no progress. 5. Tests for each. | <a id="h068"></a>H068 | Outbound rendering: streaming edits, tool-progress bubbles, typing/status lines, verbosity tiers | copy | Parțial | Reevaluat | The reduced set the row accepted beyond streaming is absent: no long-running 'still working, N min' message on a chat channel while a slow turn runs (\`TURN_BUSY_REPLY\` only answers a second message sent while a turn holds the session); no \`tool_progress: log\` per-turn append-only record of which tools ran; no quiet/show-tools level with a tool-progress message and its cleanup after the final answer, and no \`/verbose\`-style chat command to switch it (the slash-command registry has help, status, sessions, pause, stop, resume, jobs, remind and voice); no typing indicator on an ordinary text turn; no single RO/EN reply-string catalog: the decision-inbox cards still hardcode Romanian labels (\`✅ Aprob\`, \`❌ Resping\`) while channel notes and slash-command replies are English. Neither Slack nor Discord implements an edit call — no \`begin_stream\`, Slack posts only through \`chat_postMessage\`, Discord has no message PATCH — although both descriptors declare \`supports_edit=True\`, so the edit path is missing as well as the approval contract below. Slack and Discord replies are not streamed until one edit stream can be approved as a single \`channel.reply\` decision (H104). The missing typing indicator on an ordinary text turn is counted once, under H683's acknowledge-at-ingest gap. | <a id="h104"></a>H104 | Live/streaming replies in chat | copy | Parțial | Reevaluat | Neither Slack nor Discord implements an edit call — no \`begin_stream\`, Slack posts only through \`chat_postMessage\`, Discord has no message PATCH — although both descriptors declare \`supports_edit=True\`, so the edit path is missing as well as the approval contract below. Slack and Discord replies are not streamed: both descriptors declare \`supports_edit\`, but \`_begin_channel_draft\` refuses them because the row's governance clause requires one edit stream to be a single \`channel.reply\` approval decision and no such contract exists yet; until it does, their replies arrive whole after approval. The Matrix adapter (\`MatrixChannel\`, a webhook channel), another edit-capable platform the rationale names, posts whole \`m.room.message\` events: its descriptor does not declare \`supports_edit\` and it has no edit (\`m.replace\`) or draft path. Not delivered: reading audio files, video, video notes, animations, documents and stickers (recognised, answered with 'reading … is not wired up yet'); a disk cache under a media directory so vision tools can open a local path (bytes stay in memory for one call); photo-burst batching and media-group (album) debounce; location and venue pins (recognised as \`location\`, deliberately not echoed, not read); caching of media from observed group messages and from replied-to messages; media ingestion on WhatsApp, Discord, Teams, Google Chat and email (those adapters pass text only). Documented limitation, not solved: taint cannot be tracked through the model; what travels is the coarse fact that the turn read untrusted material, and the channel's \`inbound\` origin is what keeps an action derived from a photo in approval. \`mark_turn_recall_tainted()\` is not called here because it would be a no-op (escalate-only over an already untrusted origin). UNVERIFIED on real services: the suite is hermetic; no real Telegram message received, no local VLM actually called, \`getFile\` and the download exercised only against a fake client. ## web

| ID | Capabilitate | Decizie inițială | Stare cod | Bază | Observație / restanță |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| <a id="h129"></a>H129 | Deep-link / bookmark a UI surface (URL routing) | copy | Parțial | Reevaluat | Nerva has no global multi-profile switch or plugin route registry; profile-keyed remount and plugin-aware route extension from the full Hermes contract remain unsupported. Current route/demo-context remount does not claim multi-profile equivalence. Browser tests validate navigation with a stubbed backend, not production-host services. [d1](../frontend/src/hud-routing.ts) [d2](../frontend/src/console-routes.ts) [d3](../frontend/src/app.tsx) |
| <a id="h130"></a>H130 | Serve the UI under a reverse-proxy sub-path | copy | Echivalent | Reevaluat | Același bundle SPA funcționează la rădăcină sau sub un JARVIS_ROOT_PATH validat, cu reverse proxy care elimină prefixul. Bootstrap-ul serverului, navigarea și istoricul, API/SSE/streaming, chunk-urile lazy, fonturile, manifestul și service worker-ul folosesc prefixul; cache-urile PWA sunt separate per prefix. Paginile /map și /mission-control legate din SPA sunt adaptate. Teste prin aplicația reală și proxy HTTP verifică reload, shortcut-uri, cache offline, auth pe POST și lipsa cererilor la rădăcina greșită; coliziunile /v2 și /api au regresii. Sub prefix, /metrics păstrează eticheta de rută (middleware-ul de prefix întoarce scope\["route"\] către golden-signals, la fiecare mesaj trimis și la eroare), iar căile nerutate rămân \<unmatched\>. Contractul este pentru browser cu proxy prefix-stripping: wrapper-ul nativ cere prefix gol, /v1 și proxy-urile care păstrează prefixul nu sunt incluse. Fixture-ul dezactivează lifespan/provider startup; nu se pretinde deployment sau probă cu provider live. | <a id="h135"></a>H135 | Appearance preference follows the owner across devices | copy | Echivalent | Reevaluat | Browser HUD, ambient and responsive phone layouts synchronize fixed appearance preferences through a narrow user-guarded Settings DB document. Boot reads never write defaults; stale known values coerce to supported choices. Prefix-scoped immutable edit journals preserve offline changes across tabs, server revision CAS prevents stale writes without Web Locks, and acknowledgements remove exact edits even after unmount. Motion defaults to the OS System preference. Two independent real browser contexts recover saved choices after cache loss. Five fixed local UI font choices now share that normalization, revision and offline journal contract. The HUD applies the body/display override at its root; code and explicitly monospace elements retain their independent font stack. Real root/prefix browser tests verify computed families, persistence in a fresh browser, look independence, offline replay and code font invariance. The separate native application now reads the same owner Settings DB document at boot and foreground entry. Its connection-scoped finite cache, explicit-save hydration epochs and serialized current-record writes prevent stale credentials or appearance from returning. All 19 native consumers react without resetting local component state; code fonts remain independent. The accepted cross-client preference contract is implemented; native authoring and a full curated font catalog are not required by this row. Scope remains one configured owner instance. Unsupported native fonts have explicit platform fallbacks; unavailable storage cannot preserve offline choices across process restarts. Native rendering is covered with real React source and host mocks, not device GUI proof. [d1](../agents/core/appearance.py) [d2](../agents/core/routers/preferences.py) [d3](../agents/core/settings_db.py) |

| <a id="h156"></a>H156 | Edit an agent's persona and description from the UI | update | Parțial | Reevaluat | (1) No write path to the live persona. Nothing writes a committed or rolled-back version into the SOUL.local.md that \`soul_path_for\` resolves. A commit in PromptsPanel still changes nothing the agent says until someone edits the file by hand. The H672 refresh reads only that file, never the version store. It fires only when a session's context compaction commits, so it is not a reload the editor can trigger; with no compaction, the edit still waits for a restart. (2) The version store is not seeded from the on-disk SOUL, so its history is empty for any persona never committed through the panel. (3) The commit and rollback routes write no audit event, but the governance requires SOUL edits in the audit log. (4) No description editor and no Auto-generate. \`PUT /api/admin/agents/{agent_id}\` (agents/core/routers/admin.py:448) only stores display overrides in an in-memory dict, and nothing drafts a description with a model for the owner to accept. (5) The Dossier has no Edit action that opens the current SOUL in the versioned editor. [d1](../agents/core/routers/agents_api.py) [d2](../agents/core/agent.py) [d3](../agents/core/orchestrator.py) |
| <a id="h157"></a>H157 | Edit every configuration key from the UI | update | Parțial | Reevaluat | (1) No cross-key search over every setting: SettingsPanel has no filter, and the /admin search reaches only the 61 keys in its SECTIONS table, not all 160. The six new \`llm.openrouter_\*\` keys, for example, are reachable only from SettingsPanel and \`nerva config\`. (2) No export of the configuration as JSON for moving it to another box; secret-kind values would need masking or exclusion. (3) No import: no route takes a JSON document, runs every key through \`validate_category\` first, and then does an all-or-nothing write with an audit row. (4) No per-category reset: only the global \`/api/admin/settings/reseed\` exists, and it writes no audit row either. [d1](../agents/core/routers/admin.py) [d2](../agents/core/settings_db.py) [d3](../frontend/src/gap.tsx) |

| <a id="h161"></a>H161 | Warn when the box is running out of memory or disk | copy | Parțial | Reevaluat | Missing pieces: (1) The banner. Nothing in frontend/src renders a memory/disk pressure banner, ranks the five conditions (disk critical, memory critical, suspected OOM restart, disk elevated, memory elevated) or shows only the worst undismissed one. Pressure appears only as scrolling ticker lines and autonomy-queue alert tasks. (2) Suspected OOM restart. Nothing reads a boot id, the previous process's exit state or kernel OOM evidence; agents/ has no boot_id, dmesg or oom-kill references outside unrelated modules. (3) Dismissal. There is no dismissal at all, so none is keyed by boot id or re-armed after a restart. (4) Clearing on confirmed recovery. evaluate flips to healthy on the first healthy sample (no hysteresis), so a flapping reading clears and re-raises. Sampling also stops entirely when autonomy.mode is off (autonomy_coordinator.py:302) or ESTOP is engaged (autonomy_coordinator.py:257), which leaves the warning dependent on autonomy being on. The disk probe watches only '/' by default, not the data directory's volume. [d1](../agents/core/autonomy/observer.py) [d2](../agents/core/autonomy_coordinator.py) [d3](../agents/core/orchestrator.py) |

(2) A single confirm primitive whose severity tiers mirror RiskTier: reversible gets a one-click or two-step confirm, irreversible/money a typed confirmation. It must replace the hand-rolled confirmations in KillSwitchPanel (including disengage), forget-me, acquisition purge, marketplace removal, trust-ops rotation and the ESTOP pause, so the UI and the approval queue describe risk the same way. An owner still cannot create or remove a webhook without hand-crafting an admin HTTP request. Five accepted parts are missing. (1) A create dialog: agent/workflow target, name, signed toggle. (2) A created-result view that shows the trigger URL and the token or signing_secret exactly once, with copy, and never again. (3) A detail pane: target, target_type, signed, calls, last_called, token_hint. (4) A delete control with confirmation. (5) The governance half: create_webhook and delete_webhook write no audit record. The precedent is _audit_settings_change in agents/core/routers/admin.py:90-98 (audit.log of a SecurityEvent, no secret values). The Hermes receiver-disabled and restart-gateway banners are not needed, because the router is always mounted. [d1](../agents/core/routers/webhooks.py) [d2](../agents/core/webhooks.py) [d3](../frontend/src/modes2.tsx) |

Every field therefore renders '—', and no per-agent or per-model breakdown is shown. /api/cost, /api/analytics/cost and /api/analytics/model-tiers have no HUD caller. No surface shows a local-vs-cloud split of spend or tokens: the %-local meter counts runs only. The Command Center has no Usage section. There is no per-session context/usage meter (tokens, context used/max/percent, cost): the /chat ChatResponse in agents/web.py carries only reply and pending_approvals, and the CLI usage report hard-codes cost_basis 'unavailable' (agents/cli/nerva.py:1298). The trust-status \`mic\` value is a static env-flag mute switch, not a live 'I am listening' signal. The indicator must stay read-only: turning the mic on is a device permission, not a control on this surface. | <a id="h242"></a>H242 | Workspace/project model, repo discovery, strict setup verification and diagnostics bundle | update | Echivalent | Reevaluat | Nerva copies Hermes's setup.runtime_check into the owner's terminal. The hub resolves the route with the same llm_router.select_backend call a Jarvis turn makes (agents/core/routers/onboarding.py:241) and names its verdict: a local provider/model pair must be resident, and a cloud route (Gemini, Claude, or the cloud-compatible adapter set by llm.compatible_provider) counts only when the router handed back the very backend that route names (agents/core/routers/onboarding.py:142, agents/core/routers/onboarding.py:278-281). A real HybridRouter pins this for the local, cloud-flash, claude and both cloud-compatible paths (tests/test_first_run_command_center.py:493). \`nerva doctor\` (and \`python scripts/doctor.py\`) adds the advisory runtime_resolves row: one GET of /api/onboarding/command-center at the hub \`nerva status\` reads (scripts/doctor.py:208), run only after /readyz answered at that same address and otherwise reported as skip, never ok (scripts/doctor.py:287-291). It sends one credential, the user token when both are set (scripts/doctor.py:255-262), never prints it, and is ok only on a ready verdict that names the route, provider and model (scripts/doctor.py:333-337). A refused token, a reply cut off mid-body, a listener that does not speak HTTP, a bad URL or an odd resident list is a named warn, not a traceback (scripts/doctor.py:313-318; tests/test_doctor.py:433, tests/test_doctor.py:458). \`nerva status\` now labels the old any-model state 'resident' and adds a runnable: line from the same verdict (agents/cli/nerva.py:519); a timeout or reset on that read shows 'unknown — hub_unreachable' and the rest of the status still prints (agents/cli/nerva.py:529). A reply cut off mid-body or a garbled status line on any hub read is HubUnavailable, so \`nerva status\` reports an unreachable hub instead of a traceback (agents/cli/client.py:102, tests/test_nerva_cli.py:179). Both tools dial a wildcard bind (0.0.0.0, ::) over loopback (agents/cli/client.py:53). An end-to-end test drives both terminal checks against the real app (tests/test_first_run_command_center.py:550). Diagnostics stay local: the redacted bundle is served over one admin GET (agents/core/routers/support.py:21), and neither module imports a network client (tests/test_support_bundle.py:67), so the share_nous upload stays refused. The projects/workspaces model was skipped by decision. Known limits the row does not require: the verdict is for the route a short Jarvis chat takes; and a cloud route is judged by the router's own selection, not by a live call. [d1](../agents/core/routers/onboarding.py) [d2](../scripts/doctor.py) [d3](../agents/cli/nerva.py) |
| <a id="h243"></a>H243 | Completions (filesystem paths, slash commands) and provider/model options (list, save key, disconnect) | update | Parțial | Reevaluat | (1) No provider options: no route returns models grouped by provider (cloud and local) with cost hints, and cost_tracker's price table is not shown per model. (2) No save-key: no admin route writes a provider API key to the secret store and returns the refreshed model list. The pools read env vars only, so a key saved at runtime would never reach the router without a pool rebuild. (3) No disconnect: no route removes a provider's credential and rebuilds its pool, so there is no single place to revoke a provider. No audit event exists for either write. \`/api/secrets/broker\` (agents/core/routers/secrets.py:29, agents/core/routers/secrets.py:56) stores action-time \`{{secret:NAME}}\` values, not the router's provider keys. (4) No slash completion: neither the chat composer nor QuickbarPanel completes command names or their \`usage\` arguments from \`/api/commands\` as the user types. No status call answers who holds the mic. Arming does not pass the capture-consent policy the governance requires; the policy that exists (mobile/src/screens/capturePolicy.ts) covers passive capture only. Arming also writes no audit event, and the host pipeline starts at boot with neither check. The separate open item that no install path installs a voice engine is outside this row. There is no persisted last-run marker and no min-interval gate shared across processes; the sweep is a fixed daily cron in each scheduler process. Changes to retention TTLs or the enabled flag are ordinary admin PUT /api/admin/settings/retention writes. The governance field requires them to go through the irreversible approval tier instead. | <a id="h270"></a>H270 | Writer allowlist for names that hijack execution or soften posture | copy | Parțial | Reevaluat | Nerva has no write-side policy for environment-variable names — the row's deliverable (a declared-names allowlist, depends_on "the key registry") does not exist, and the row defers it rather than excluding it. #1179 changed nothing on this row's subject: its client.py change is an outbound disclosure (cwd_secret_files :215-242 and the "withholding is undercut" WARNING in _merged_env :561-572), not a name policy or a writer. Neither did the fail-closed tool-filter bounds change (56903ac1: tool_patterns_over_bounds, a deny-bounds block in MCPServer.__init__ and a tools_allow/tools_deny bounds refusal in admin_mcp_add) — it touches no env name, writer or override path and only moved line numbers. H502 (#1174) did change item 3 and half of item 2, and that is folded in here. Three items owed, two shippable today against real surfaces: 1. THE REGISTRY (the row's core item, blocked on its stated dependency). No ENV_VAR_NAME allowlist/denylist exists anywhere (grep for ENV_VAR_NAME/ENV_WRITE/WRITE_DENYLIST/writable_env across agents/, scripts/, tests/, frontend/src/ is empty at HEAD) and no \`config.write\` capability kind exists in agents/core/capability_actions.py. Of Hermes' hijack names, LD_PRELOAD, LD_LIBRARY_PATH, DYLD_\*, PYTHONHOME, PYTHONSTARTUP, NODE_OPTIONS, GIT_SSH_COMMAND and PAGER are named nowhere in code or tests — but PATH, SHELL (agents/core/mcp/client.py:162), NODE_PATH (:171) and PATH, SHELL, PYTHONPATH (agents/core/environments/__init__.py:18, :27, :30) ARE named, as members of the two OUTBOUND allowlists, and EDITOR is exercised at tests/test_nerva_import.py:165 (pinned dropped at tests/test_nerva_import.py:825). Design consequence: the outbound lists cannot double as the write-side registry — they deliberately admit the plumbing names Hermes forbids on write — so the registry is a separate, inbound-only list. When any env/config writer lands, that allowlist must land in the SAME PR, refusing an undeclared name before authorization — the shape of agents/core/capability_actions.py:139-143 refusing missing_inputs before the authorizer at :151-152. 2. THE MCP \`env\` BOUNDARY IS UNREFUSED (shippable now; the "untested" half closed with #1174). MCPServerConfig (agents/core/routers/mcp.py:128-139) sets no \`model_config\`, so pydantic's default extra="ignore" makes \`POST /api/admin/mcp {"env": {"LD_PRELOAD": "/tmp/evil.so"}}\` return 200 and silently drop the key instead of refusing it — reproduced end to end at HEAD through TestClient (200 {"ok": true}; stored MCPServer.env == {}; to_config() has no env key). The field it would populate is live — MCPServer.env (agents/core/mcp/client.py:424) → _merged_env (:531-577) → create_subprocess_exec(env=…) (:453-460). Already pinned by tests/test_mcp_transports.py:507-522: \`env\` is not a MCPServerConfig field, to_config (:798-812) does not export it and load_from_config (:814-849) does not read it. Reachability: the HUD only GETs /api/admin/mcp (frontend/src/api/live.ts:397) and no CLI adds servers, so the raw admin API is the only path today. Still owed: \`extra="forbid"\` (or an explicit named refusal) on MCPServerConfig, plus a test asserting the route refuses an \`env\` body rather than swallowing it — and the same refusal must cover the hijack names above on the day \`env\` becomes operator-settable. 3. PER-SERVER \`env\` BYPASSES THE ALLOWLIST IN BOTH BRANCHES. stdio_env_baseline applies overrides unfiltered after the allowlist loop (agents/core/mcp/client.py:278-279; docstring :253 "Explicit per-server overrides always win"), and the legacy branch does \`merged = os.environ.copy(); merged.update(self.env)\` with no filter (:575-576). The "decide whether the baseline should become the default" half of this item is DONE: since H502 the baseline is on unless JARVIS_MCP_STDIO_ENV_BASELINE=0 (:179-190; docs/FLAGS.md "on by default since H502") and tests/test_mcp_transports.py:386-421 pins both directions, so the legacy branch is now the explicit escape hatch rather than the shipped path. Owed: gate per-server overrides on the item-1 write-side registry (NOT merely on STDIO_ENV_ALLOWLIST + _SECRET_LIKE — that policy passes PATH, SHELL, NODE_PATH and would pass an operator-supplied PATH/NODE_PATH override untouched), or make the bypass an explicitly reviewed, audited exception. Severity, checked directly rather than inferred: LATENT. \`MCPServerConfig\` has no \`env\` field, the admin route constructs MCPServer without one (agents/core/routers/mcp.py:177-189), and the only production caller passing an override is Nerva's own WorldView writer with an in-repo secret plus the WORLDVIEW_HOST_ENV names (agents/core/mcp/worldview_write.py:222-227, :49-56). No untrusted input reaches the override loop, so this is a boundary to close before \`env\` becomes operator-settable, not a live hole. The outbound allowlist's one intentional exception — a name the owner lists in JARVIS_MCP_STDIO_ALLOWED_ENV passes even when credential-shaped (:256-260, :272-274; tests :449-503) — is owner-declared and host-wide, and is not this row's write boundary. | <a id="h288"></a>H288 | Scheduled and queued work, and its delivery destination | update | Parțial | Reevaluat | Three cron-lane clauses of Hermes' contract are unmet. The Kanban claim-lease and crash-grace asks are done. 1. HERMES_CRON_TIMEOUT: the plain cron path has no per-run wall clock. \`JobRunner._fire_once\` → \`_execute\` → \`_ask\`/\`_brief\`/\`_deliver\` (agents/core/autonomy/jobs.py:1107-1163, 1209-1230, 1351-1412) has no \`asyncio.wait_for\` at any level. In particular, \`_send_tracked\`/\`_send\` (jobs.py:1379-1412) awaits the channel adapter bare. A hung Telegram/Discord send therefore holds the job's gate stripe (jobs_dispatch.py:33-48) and its APScheduler \`max_instances=1\` slot, and nothing breaks it. The script path already does this correctly: \`asyncio.wait_for(self.runner._ask(...), 120)\` at agents/core/autonomy/jobs_scripts.py:405 and \`asyncio.wait_for(self.runner._send_tracked(...), 30)\` at :455. The fix is to apply the same bound to the cron path, ideally as a \`jobs.run_timeout_seconds\` setting next to the existing \`jobs.media_send_timeout_seconds\`. The overrun should be recorded as a normal failed run through \`_failed\`, so the 3-strike pause and the incident path still apply. 2. \`JARVIS_TASK_MAX_SECONDS\` defaults to 0, which means unbounded (agents/core/autonomy/executor.py:39-42, agents/core/autonomy_coordinator.py:1117-1120). Out of the box, a hung in-process queued task is bounded only by the 3600 s stuck-running reaper. Fix: give it a non-zero shipped default, or promote it to a validated \`autonomy.\*\` setting like \`running_ttl_seconds\`, and add a test asserting the default is finite. 3. HERMES_CRON_SESSION_DB_TIMEOUT: the jobs store opens sqlite with neither \`timeout=\` nor \`PRAGMA busy_timeout\` (agents/core/autonomy/jobs.py:508-511). The task queue sets \`timeout=30.0\` + \`PRAGMA busy_timeout=30000\` (agents/core/autonomy/queue.py:212-214). jobs.db writers therefore fall back to SQLite's 5 s default, on a WAL shared by the gateway drain loop and cron firings. One-line parity fix. Out of scope, not gaps: the row's own rationale excludes the delivery-destination knobs (_AUTO_DELIVER_PLATFORM / _CHAT_ID / _THREAD_ID, TELEGRAM_CRON_THREAD_ID). The residual ~14-knob multi-agent Kanban board (_BOARD / _WORKSPACES_ROOT / _ATTACHMENTS_ROOT / _GOAL_MODE / _DISPATCH_IN_GATEWAY) is a product decision about whether Nerva grows a shared task board, not a slice of this row. [d1](../agents/core/autonomy/queue.py) [d2](../agents/core/autonomy/worker.py) [d3](../agents/core/autonomy/executor.py) |

| <a id="h297"></a>H297 | Execute a named tool call and return a bounded, well-formed result | keep | Echivalent | Audit 07.09 | Preluat din auditul 07.09; nereevaluat în această livrare.  |
| <a id="h298"></a>H298 | Keep a huge tool result out of context without losing it | update | Echivalent | Reevaluat | The complete frozen tool-result contract is wired into real tool turns: - the threshold precedence: pinned file_read infinity (PINNED_THRESHOLDS keyed on READ_TOOL), then owner override, the MCP family, tool declaration and the default threshold; - per-result/per-turn scaling; - bounded previews with full-file paths, sizes and hashes; - finalized-file retention; - centralized byte/line/line-length limits. Since H661 the spill notice is an exact, followable call. \`page_recipe\` fills in \`file_read(path=…, offset=0, max_bytes=…)\` with the resolved path, the preview and execute_code stream fields carry it as text and as \`read_with\` arguments, and \`file_read\` pages from any offset and returns \`next_offset\`. The call is checked, not assumed: the store's \`read_back\` probe is asked about that exact path. The coordinator wires it to three checks: registry + job-toolset allowance, the turn's own offer, and FileTools reach. The spill directory is also a read-only door for exact spill names, and spill names never look like secrets. When file_read cannot page the file from this turn, the notice says the file is kept for the owner and does not name a dead call. Other guarantees: - Exclusive per-writer temporary files preserve concurrent stream and whole-result receipts. - A shape-truncated stream keeps its bytes even when its annotation makes the preview longer. - Upstream byte truncation and small-output discard still work. Ordinary Agent and Orchestrator tool paths snapshot configured/cached backend capacity before guardrails. They forward immutable per-turn metadata without unwrapping or bypassing generation. Known capacity and resolved job bounds constrain the completion reserve, initial and later transcripts, schemas, structured tool arguments and finite result thresholds, and file_read never re-spills. Invalid metadata refuses the tool turn. Unknown capacity, including adapters without loaded metadata, keeps the documented model-family estimate, and token accounting remains heuristic. Re-run at 55d625f: 243 cases pass across tests/test_tool_result_store.py, test_tool_loop_result_spill.py, test_environment_output_limits.py, test_code_tools.py, test_effective_tool_window.py and test_spill_paging.py. Honest limits: - A \*streamed\* spill now has a ceiling, DEFAULT_MAX_STREAM_BYTES = 5,000,000, which the code calls Hermes' own figure. Past it, the file ends in one explicit '\[... spill capped ...\]' marker, the result reports capped/kept_bytes/file_bytes, and the stream's end survives only in the inline tail. Whole-result spills are uncapped. - Retention has a newest-file exception. - Interrupted captures, orphan cleanup and arbitrary storage failure are not permanent complete-output guarantees. - This is code equivalence, not live provider/Windows containment proof. [d1](../agents/core/tool_result_store.py) [d2](../tests/test_tool_result_store.py) [d3](../agents/core/agent_runtime.py) |

| <a id="h313"></a>H313 | Speak | update | Echivalent | Reevaluat | The model can decide to say one short text aloud on one room's device, and can do it again. register_speak_tool (agents/core/voice/speak_tool.py:603) adds a ToolRPC \`speak\` tool with gated=True, trusted execution and its own gated intake (agents/core/voice/speak_tool.py:630, agents/core/voice/speak_tool.py:636). It is registered only while JARVIS_MEDIA_DIRECTOR is on (agents/core/voice/speak_tool.py:616), and AutonomyCoordinator wires it with the broker kernel and the governed enqueue (agents/core/autonomy_coordinator.py:462, agents/core/autonomy_coordinator.py:1296). It reaches the same Media Director instance the media routes use, so the owner's device registry and session board apply (agents/core/routers/media_director.py:272). SpeakTool.preflight (agents/core/voice/speak_tool.py:307) runs at proposal and again before the approved run. It refuses text over the spoken-reply cap and code-only text (agents/core/voice/speak_tool.py:313, agents/core/voice/speak_tool.py:315). It resolves a room to that room's one announce-capable default device, so the card title and the persisted args name the speaker (agents/core/voice/speak_tool.py:235, agents/core/voice/speak_tool.py:386). It raises no card the run could only refuse: unified API off, kernel off or unbound, no media root, no speech backend, no driver for the device, or no configured presence room each refuse by name (agents/core/voice/speak_tool.py:334, agents/core/voice/speak_tool.py:329). SpeakTool.intake (agents/core/voice/speak_tool.py:364) shows the kernel the exact toolrpc.speak row it enqueues. Under the production composition (worker.kernel_gate over MediationKernelBridge, agents/core/autonomy/worker.py:220, agents/core/kernel/binding.py:108) that decision binds as the row's intake evidence, and a DENY raises no card (agents/core/voice/speak_tool.py:390). Only the human-accepted durable toolrpc.speak row runs. The kind is trusted (agents/core/autonomy_coordinator.py:66) and the executor routes it to the trusted rail (agents/core/autonomy_coordinator.py:1317-1320). ToolRPC re-runs the preflight and requires the trusted execution context (agents/core/tool_rpc.py:434, agents/core/tool_rpc.py:462), and the handler refuses a row of any other kind (agents/core/voice/speak_tool.py:460). SpeakTool.execute (agents/core/voice/speak_tool.py:455) synthesizes the clip through SpokenReply on the host TTSEngine in the owner's configured voice (agents/core/voice/speak_tool.py:471, agents/core/channels/spoken_reply.py:242, agents/core/voice/tts.py:144). It writes the clip owner-only under the first media root (agents/core/voice/speak_tool.py:558, agents/core/voice/speak_tool.py:572) and presents it only through CapabilityActionAPI.perform('action:media.present') in announce mode (agents/core/voice/speak_tool.py:511, agents/core/voice/speak_tool.py:516). The facade asks the Action Kernel before the handler runs (agents/core/capability_actions.py:168) and refuses on a DENY (agents/core/capability_actions.py:175), so the kernel authorizes the effect and the tool never reaches a driver. A kernel QUEUE on that present is honoured as the accepted row and audited as speak.durably_approved (agents/core/voice/speak_tool.py:419, agents/core/voice/speak_tool.py:441). A DENY becomes kernel_denied with the kernel's reason (agents/core/voice/speak_tool.py:533). The Media Director treats an announce as a one-shot notice (agents/core/media_director.py:738): it never blocks the next present and costs no interrupt budget (agents/core/media_director.py:890), and whatever it cut into stays the restore point (agents/core/media_director.py:992). Because the tool is gated, tool_profiles offers it only to the owner on the operator surface (agents/core/tool_profiles.py:167, agents/core/tool_profiles.py:171), and the snapshot pins this (tests/_snapshots/tool_profiles.json:39, tests/test_tool_profiles.py:202). Tests cover: registration and postures (tests/test_speak_tool.py:170, tests/test_speak_tool.py:196); named preflight refusals that enqueue nothing (tests/test_speak_tool.py:271, tests/test_speak_tool.py:320); and the card naming the device (tests/test_speak_tool.py:286). With the real kernel.authorize, AutonomyPolicy and KillSwitch behind worker.kernel_gate, an approved speak is mediated and audited, and an engaged kill switch refuses both the run and the card (tests/test_speak_tool.py:520, tests/test_speak_tool.py:543, tests/test_speak_tool.py:576). Three back-to-back normal speaks on one device all play at no budget cost (tests/test_speak_tool.py:591). Speaking over music needs high urgency, pays once and keeps the music as the restore point (tests/test_speak_tool.py:608, tests/test_media_director.py:762). A kernel DENY, a missing TTS backend, a missing media root and a driverless device each refuse by name with no playback, and an engine that yields no audio refuses as tts_failed with nothing presented (tests/test_speak_tool.py:681, tests/test_speak_tool.py:469, tests/test_speak_tool.py:666, tests/test_speak_tool.py:703, tests/test_speak_tool.py:716). A row of another kind cannot speak (tests/test_speak_tool.py:753), and the clip spool stays bounded (tests/test_speak_tool.py:779). Known limits outside the accepted adaptation: an accepted speak runs at the next autonomy tick (default 60 s, floor 15 s, agents/core/autonomy_coordinator.py:253), like every gated tool. A presence:auto card names the sentinel, because only play time knows whether the owner is in the presence room. Audible output needs an owner-wired speaker driver (the in-repo NullMediaDriver refuses and LocalFileMediaDriver is a reference), the same host seam as every media.present. The speech-backend gate needs edge-tts or Kokoro installed, like the spoken-reply channel. An announce of any length, including one sent through POST /api/media/present, no longer holds its device, as docs/FLAGS.md states. Hermes's tts.provider, custom command providers and tts.speed are outside the accepted adaptation, because Nerva's TTS subsystem was already ahead. [d1](../agents/core/voice/speak_tool.py) [d2](../agents/core/autonomy_coordinator.py) [d3](../agents/core/media_director.py) |

| <a id="h344"></a>H344 | Reading skills from directories shared with other AI tools | update | Echivalent | Reevaluat | Import-once stays the only way a Hermes / OpenClaw / Claude Code skill enters Nerva's skills tree (no live-scanned external_dirs tier; SkillLoader.discover() is unchanged), and a re-run of \`python scripts/nerva_import.py\` now notices upstream movement instead of reporting a blanket skipped/exists. Each imported skill is re-detected against the absolute source_path and sha256 recorded in its manifest.json (importer.py:946, importer.py:991): unchanged; changed, with the old and new digests plus a bounded line diff shown by --json; moved to another folder of the same install and followed (importer.py:925); removed; renamed; unreadable; or source_untrusted when a record names a file outside the install, which is then never read (importer.py:1273). The default run is a dry run. \`--apply --update\` re-imports only changed skills that this source imported (importer.py:1056): the current copy is backed up under data/imports/skill_backups, PENDING_REVIEW is written before the new bytes land, SKILL.sig and the legacy approval marker are cleared, and the owner's approval is revoked. A linked control file, an unusable backup dir or a refused save fails closed, and the write is bound to the probed digest. Every import and re-import crosses skill.install through the real Action Kernel at the shipped CLI (nerva_import.py:577, bound at nerva_import.py:1032 and as the ImportRunner default at nerva_import.py:682), behind SKILL_IMPORT_CONTRACT (nerva_import.py:145). The persisted kill switch is re-read on every decision, so an engaged halt refuses an import or re-import before a byte moves. The external origin keeps a policy GRANT at QUEUE, and only GRANT or QUEUE writes (nerva_import.py:764), so every write lands quarantined. The human summary names a local edit that --update replaces (with its backup path), a failed revoke, and moved, untrusted and refused rows (nerva_import.py:938). Tests in test_nerva_import.py: 44 passed and 1 live-gated skip under python 3.11 and 3.12. They include test_cli_skill_writes_cross_the_real_kernel_and_honour_the_kill_switch, test_runner_default_authorizer_is_the_kernel_for_skills_and_queue_for_memory, test_source_moved_within_the_install_is_followed_not_reported_removed, test_apply_update_reimports_changed_skill_into_quarantine_and_revokes_approval, test_rescan_never_follows_a_record_outside_the_install and test_reimport_refuses_a_linked_control_file_and_never_writes_through_it. Two known limits sit outside the accepted requirement. (1) The CLI binds no durable audit sink, so each kernel decision is recorded only on its report row (kernel/kernel_reason) and in the in-process kernel metrics. (2) A record written before this change with a cwd-relative source_path (relative --home) is not rewritten by an unchanged re-run. From another directory it reads skipped/exists plus source_untrusted until a changed source is re-imported with --apply --update, which records the absolute path. [d1](../agents/core/skills/importer.py) [d2](../scripts/nerva_import.py) [d3](../tests/test_nerva_import.py) |

| <a id="h351"></a>H351 | Prompt-injection warnings when a skill is loaded | update | Parțial | Reevaluat | \*\*This is not the surface H351 asks for.\*\* The row wants a \*load-time\* warning on the skill BODY: a skill whose file sits outside the trusted roots, or whose content matches \`_INJECTION_PATTERNS\`, flagged in the \`skill_view\` result so the model sees the warning beside the content, with the body fenced as data the way \`rag_guard\` fences recall. None of that is here, and #1179 did not add it. This slice hardens the \*discovery\* block — the row's declared dependency ("model-facing discovery, row 1") — so a manifest cannot address the model through the skills index. Nerva has no \`skill_view\` equivalent: a skill body reaches the model with nothing re-checking it after import and nothing spotlighting or datamarking it. \*\*What the two gates are worth, stated plainly rather than implied.\*\* The signature gate detects corruption and accidental drift in a skill that ships a sidecar; it does not stop an attacker. Anyone with write access to a skill directory — exactly the access needed to edit SKILL.md — deletes \`SKILL.sig\` in the same operation, the skill verifies as \`unsigned\`, and it is advertised again on its new description. That evasion is pinned by a test on purpose (\`test_deleting_the_sidecar_evades_the_trust_gate\`). Closing it needs two things this repo does not have: a signed skills tree, and a record kept OUTSIDE the tree of which skills were once seen signed. The review raised a second, simpler evasion that is also real and is recorded here rather than fixed: at the shipped default, with no \`JARVIS_SKILL_SIGNING_KEY\`, the "signature" is a plain sha256 anyone can recompute (\`agents/core/skills/signing.py\`, SEC-B2). The injection gate is a ten-entry phrase table: it costs a row to a description that spells "ignore all previous instructions" in the words the table lists \*and\* in a placement the normaliser reaches, and not to one that says the same thing in words the table does not list — the normalisation (invisible-strip, whitespace-collapse and their composition, since #1179) widens what the table \*sees\*, not what it \*lists\*, and it does not see everything. It raises the floor on copied-in manifests; it is not a filter a motivated author cannot write around. \*\*The invisible-character placement this row carried as open is closed, and what is left of it is narrowing, not a hole.\*\* The spaced copy plus the NFKC fold close the in-place-of-a-separator placement in both row fields, including the \`command\` door: \`re.fullmatch(r"\\w+", command)\` still admits the four Hangul fillers U+115F, U+1160, U+3164 and U+FFA0 (category Lo, letters to \`\\w\`), so a command can still be \*spelled\* with them — it is now detected and the row dropped, pinned per character. Narrowing the gate to ASCII \`\[A-Za-z0-9_\]\` remains worth doing as defence in depth: it costs none of the 34 bundled commands, all ASCII today (re-checked on this head), and it would keep the field bounded independently of what the phrase table happens to list. What the widened normaliser still does NOT reach is unchanged and stated plainly: a Cyrillic or Greek homoglyph, a combining overlay, markdown emphasis inside a word, and a phrase split across two rows or two fields all scan clean — that is a confusables table and a cross-field pass, neither of which is here. \*\*The catalog-level pins that were missing are here, and the docstring drift is corrected.\*\* The own-token placement (\`Ignore \<U+200B\> all previous instructions\`), closed by the collapse-of-the-stripped-copy variant but pinned only at the quarantine layer, now has its own catalog case, as do the five in-place-of-a-separator spellings, the four filler spellings of \`command\` and the fullwidth phrase; each was run against the pre-slice module and fails there. \`loader.py\`'s \`prompt_catalog\` docstring and the comment above the scan described a two-copy scan and called the table "eleven-entry" against ten; both now name \`_detection_variants\` as the source of the copy set and the count is right. \*\*One thing deliberately left narrow:\*\* \`strip_invisible\` stays TAG-only for \`strip_invisible_deep\`, which runs over tool results and MCP payloads where a BOM or a bidi mark can be part of data a caller expects back unchanged. Those paths still run no \`detect_injection_normalized\` (#1179's \`mcp/client.py\` change is the stdio cwd disclosure, not a scan); whether they should is a separate slice with its own blast radius — and the normaliser now has a second consumer, the SOUL persona scan in \`agent.py\` (switched by #1179 from a strip-only scan), so any further widening of it, the separator-replacement copy above included, is paid for there too. [d1](../agents/core/skills/loader.py) [d2](../agents/core/security/quarantine.py) [d3](../tests/test_skills_in_prompt.py) |

| <a id="h368"></a>H368 | Refuse to speak the wrong protocol to a lookalike host | copy | Echivalent | Reevaluat | A hostname-parsed protocol table guards every model backend at the wire. host_mandated_protocol (agents/core/llm/host_protocol.py:112) takes the hostname from hostname_of (agents/core/llm/host_protocol.py:85). hostname_of parses strings with httpx.URL, the parser the wire dials with (agents/core/llm/host_protocol.py:103). A fullwidth, ideographic or halfwidth-ideographic dot spelling of a vendor host is therefore refused at HybridRouter.detect() as well as at the wire. urlsplit remains the fallback for text httpx rejects, and httpx cannot dial that text either. The host is lower-cased with the trailing dot dropped, then matched exactly or as whole labels (agents/core/llm/host_protocol.py:62-69): api.anthropic.com -\> anthropic_messages; api.openai.com and one regional label -\> openai; bedrock-runtime\[-fips\].\<region\>.amazonaws.com\[.cn\] -\> bedrock_converse. Lookalike suffixes, path spoofs and userinfo spoofs mandate nothing. Each backend's ledger name declares its protocol (agents/core/llm/host_protocol.py:49). Every llm_async_client caller on this head is declared: anthropic, gemini (gemini_cache included), openai-responses, xai, openrouter, lm-studio, ollama and vlm. protocol_refusal (agents/core/llm/host_protocol.py:131) also refuses an undeclared backend on a mandated host. The check lives in the egress-ledger request hook that every backend's llm_async_client carries (agents/core/llm/egress.py:67-70), so it runs on every redirect hop. A mismatch records an allowed=False ledger row with the reason and raises before the transport is touched, so neither the body nor the auth header leaves. HybridRouter.detect refuses a compatible base URL on a host that mandates another protocol (agents/core/llm/hybrid_router.py:375); the refusal is logged and the backend is not built. HostProtocolRefused subclasses Exception, neither OSError nor httpx.HTTPError (agents/core/llm/host_protocol.py:73), so no connection-failure handler reads a refusal as 'backend down'. OllamaBackend.resolve_context_window logs a warning and returns unknown on a refusal (agents/core/llm/base.py:720), instead of raising out of compaction and route planning. Adaptation from Hermes: Nerva has no per-session api_mode that can go stale, so the mandate refuses a mismatched backend rather than re-picking a protocol. Nerva's OpenAI family admits both OpenAI dialects (chat completions and Responses) at api.openai.com. Base URLs remain env-only until the named-custom-endpoints row exists, and the wire guard covers any base URL unchanged. Coverage is tests/test_host_mandated_protocol.py (43 tests): - vendor hosts, including the 5 IDNA-dot spellings (tests/test_host_mandated_protocol.py:61); - 15 lookalike, path, userinfo and other hosts (tests/test_host_mandated_protocol.py:82); - an OpenAI-protocol backend aimed at api.anthropic.com sends zero requests and leaves two blocked ledger rows (tests/test_host_mandated_protocol.py:119); - the client raises before dispatch (tests/test_host_mandated_protocol.py:141); - matching and unmandated hosts still flow (tests/test_host_mandated_protocol.py:151); - a redirect onto a vendor host is refused per hop (tests/test_host_mandated_protocol.py:164); - router refusals, including both IDNA spellings (tests/test_host_mandated_protocol.py:204, tests/test_host_mandated_protocol.py:213, tests/test_host_mandated_protocol.py:234); - the default and custom endpoints are still built (tests/test_host_mandated_protocol.py:222); - the refusal is neither an OSError nor an httpx.HTTPError (tests/test_host_mandated_protocol.py:247); - the Ollama probe degrades and leaves a blocked ledger row (tests/test_host_mandated_protocol.py:254). All wire tests use the real client, with httpx.MockTransport injected through llm_async_client's public transport kwarg. [d1](../agents/core/llm/host_protocol.py) [d2](../agents/core/llm/egress.py) [d3](../agents/core/llm/hybrid_router.py) |

The H583 data_collection knob covers only OpenRouter's upstream routing; the Claude, Gemini, xAI, openai-compatible and openai-responses routes carry no data-policy fact. There is no single selection-guard registry run server-side before persistence on every model-selection surface. ## agent-core

| ID | Capabilitate | Decizie inițială | Stare cod | Bază | Observație / restanță |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| <a id="h386"></a>H386 | Session-stable, tiered system prompt with a provider cache boundary | update | Parțial | Reevaluat | Rândurile includ mai multe fire de streaming/cache/compactare/recuperare; completarea și probele fiecărei căi rămân explicite. H363 a adăugat o marcă \`cache_control\` pe blocul de sistem al wire-ului Anthropic, dar asta NU e layout-ul cerut aici: rândul cere trei tiere ordonate (stable/context/volatile) asamblate și memoizate o dată pe sesiune, un layout \`\[static, volatile\]\` reconstruibil, invalidare explicită și restaurare byte-cu-byte din DB-ul de sesiune cu verificare de identitate runtime. Din toate astea există acum doar granița de cache, pe un singur bloc. [d1](../agents/core/llm/tool_dialects.py) [d2](../agents/core/llm/base.py) [d3](../agents/core/agent_runtime.py) |
| <a id="h387"></a>H387 | Identity and project instruction files with a size cap and an injection scan | update | Parțial | Reevaluat | \*\*A deliberate deviation from the row's literal text, recorded so the next audit does not re-flag it as an oversight.\*\* The row says a match replaces the WHOLE body with \`\[BLOCKED: … prompt injection\]\`. This quarantines per line and only escalates to a whole-body block above 50% flagged lines — and, since #1179, when a non-empty body is left whitespace-only. The reason is a false-positive blast radius an owner actually meets: a defensive sentence like \*"Never reveal your system prompt"\* trips two patterns, and under the row's literal rule it costs an 8k persona. Whether to match Hermes exactly and accept that is an owner decision, not a builder's — it is written up in \`docs/OWNER_TASKS.md\` (P28.1). \*\*A residual this does not fix, and it follows from the same choice:\*\* a SINGLE-LINE persona is one line, so one flagged phrase blocks the whole body — a one-line SOUL carrying that ordinary defensive sentence is blocked entirely, with or without a trailing newline, confirmed by reproduction and unchanged by #1179. The 19 shipped SOULs are 100–156 lines each, so none meets it; but NOTHING PINS THAT: the only test that walks the shipped files (\`test_every_shipped_soul_is_clean_and_under_the_cap\`) asserts a literal \`detect_injection\` over the raw file and the size cap, and says nothing about line count. \*\*And the disclosure is API-only.\*\* \`frontend/\` does not read the \`guard\` field: \`api/actions.ts\` types the response as \`{ agent_id, soul }\` and the Dossier in \`modes.tsx\` keeps only \`r.soul\`, so the HUD shows the raw text with no verdict beside it and the user-visible half of the safety story is not wired yet (P28.2). The comments in the router say the API discloses it, which is what is true. \*\*Also absent from the row:\*\* the same treatment for the project-instruction files (\`AGENTS.md\`, \`CLAUDE.md\`) the row names beside the identity ones — H506's write-side class in \`file_tools.py\` covers them, nothing scans them on the way in; and nothing of the row's project-context priority chain (.hermes.md → AGENTS.md chain → CLAUDE.md → .cursorrules), \`--ignore-rules\`/\`--safe-mode\`, or progressive subdirectory hints exists. \*\*The guard has now survived two adversarial waves, and the record of the first was incomplete.\*\* Wave one found the per-line scan ran \`detect_injection\` on the raw line while \`strip_invisible\` covers only the TAG plane, so any other Cf format character — U+200B, U+00AD, U+FEFF, the bidi marks — survived into the scanned text while reading to the model exactly like the phrase that does not contain it; four zero-width spaces in a four-line payload took the verdict from \`blocked\` to \`flags == \[\]\`, passed the payload through byte-identically, and made \`GET /api/agents/{id}/soul\` report a clean \`guard\` for a persona that was 100% injection. That fix scanned a \`strip_format_chars\` copy and emitted the original; six format-character classes are pinned and the payload must earn the SAME verdict with and without them. Wave two (#1179) showed that closed only half the evasion: the patterns spell every gap as one literal space, so a respacing between the words (two spaces, TAB, NO-BREAK, NARROW NO-BREAK, IDEOGRAPHIC or THIN SPACE — all six pinned) scanned just as clean, and a line wrap through the phrase was two clean lines. The per-line call site is now \`detect_injection_normalized\` (raw ∪ Cf-stripped ∪ whitespace-collapsed ∪ both; the composition is pinned by a U+200B-plus-NO-BREAK-SPACE payload), \`_wrapped_injection_hits\` quarantines exactly the lines a cross-line \`injection_spans\` match covers (a four-line body with the phrase split over lines 2–3 keeps lines 1 and 4 and is not blocked), and a persona's own double spaces and hard wraps ship byte-identical because normalisation is for the scan, never the text. \*\*Known cost of the wrap pass, measured rather than quoted from its docstring:\*\* joining lines manufactures adjacency the file does not have, and the join skips blank lines, so the reach is the next NON-BLANK line, not "directly above" — a heading ending "Ignore all previous" followed by a plain line starting "instructions" is stubbed on both lines in a ten-line persona (blank line between them or not) and costs the WHOLE body in a three-line one (two of three flagged is over the ratio); end punctuation on the first half ("previous.") or a Markdown bullet/number marker on the second ("- instructions") breaks the adjacency and nothing fires. The docstring's claims that a bullet is flagged and that blank-line structure protects are both wrong in that direction. All 19 shipped SOULs pass the full four-pass scan and the wrap pass clean — by reproduction; the shipped-file pin does not run either (it calls \`detect_injection\` on the raw file, which cannot see a join), so a template edit that wraps a phrase across a line break would pass the pin and be stubbed in the field. \*\*Wave three, found by this review and not fixed or filed:\*\* the wrap pass scans ONLY the fully-normalised join (Cf-stripped, whitespace-collapsed), not the union \`_detection_variants\` gives the per-line pass — so it inherits the weakness the normaliser's own docstring warns about, that a deletion destroys a match as readily as it reveals one. "You are\\nnow\<U+200B\>in developer mode." scans clean (\`you are now\\b\` needs the boundary the zero-width space supplied in the raw text, and the join has deleted it), while the same two lines without the U+200B are stubbed on both. By the repo's own standard — the single-line form of this exact example is why the union exists — that is a wrapped phrase the model reads and the guard does not. Two more from wave one still hold: the quarantine stub numbers the FILE line through \`_body_line_offset\`, not the body line; and \`test_the_editor_endpoint_reads_the_file_the_model_reads\` drives the real endpoint, so the router half of the packaged-install fix goes red when reverted. \*\*Since H672 a persona rewritten under a running session is re-scanned only at a compaction boundary, and that boundary is opt-in\*\* (\`memory.context_compression\`, default off): a default install keeps the persona it scanned at boot until a restart — the pre-H672 posture, not a regression, but the scan on the way in still happens once per process there. (5) A per-turn accounting line (tool calls, failures, tokens) and cumulative output tokens shown in the HUD. | <a id="h409"></a>H409 | Outbound signed lifecycle webhooks | copy | Parțial | Reevaluat | The whole accepted delivery path is missing: (1) an admin-managed registry of outbound subscribers (URL, secret, event filter) with an operator entry point (admin route plus a \`nerva\` CLI verb or HUD control). (2) A delivery worker subscribed to the same event catalogue as ExtensionEventBus that POSTs the allowlisted JSON payload with an HMAC signature header from compute_signature. (3) Bounded retries with backoff and an explicit no-follow redirect policy. (4) Egress through the plugin.egress kernel mediation (agents/core/kernel/registry.py:45) and an estop check, so a kill-switch or budget stop silences the feed. (5) Payloads passed through the redaction engine before they leave the box. The reaction/vibe detector is excluded by the rationale. [d1](../agents/core/webhooks.py) [d2](../agents/core/extensions/events.py) [d3](../agents/core/extensions/manifest.py) |
| <a id="h410"></a>H410 | Universal secret redaction on every log record | update | Parțial | Reevaluat | (1) Two process entry points configure logging with a bare logging.basicConfig and never install the filter, so every record they write is unredacted: the headless coordinator (scripts/coordinator.py:122), which boots the full Orchestrator with channels, heartbeat, the autonomy loop and the scheduler, and the MCP stdio bridge (scripts/nerva_mcp_stdio.py:151). (2) There is no sensitive query-parameter, header or body-key catalogue for opaque tokens that match no vendor prefix. Probed through SecretRedactionFilter.redact_text, all of these pass unmasked: 'GET /cb?access_token=abcdef1234567890', an unquoted '?api_key=abcd1234efgh5678', 'client_secret=Zx81kdfLq0PaW3', 'X-Api-Key: 9f8e7d6c5b4a3f2e' and 'Authorization: Token abcd1234efgh5678ijkl'. The cause: generic_api_key requires a quoted value (agents/core/security/scanner.py:190) and only password keys match unquoted (agents/core/security/scanner.py:178). (3) The checksum-validated Romanian patterns the rationale relies on, ro_cnp and ro_iban (agents/core/security/scanner.py:287-288), belong to PIIScanner, which the log filter does not run, so a CNP or IBAN reaches the log verbatim. [d1](../agents/core/log.py) [d2](../agents/core/security/log_redaction.py) [d3](../agents/core/security/scanner.py) |

(3) Return the title from GET /sessions and render it on every human-facing session list: the HUD sessions card and the mobile resume modal. (2) An 80%-spent wrap-up nudge, injected once into the tool-loop transcript, asking the model to deliver from what it has. (3) The exhaustion protocol: tell the model exactly once, grant one final tool-less call, and force a summary from the transcript if that call yields no text, instead of returning the fixed safety-limit string. (4) Deadline and budget stops reported through the H396 exit-reason vocabulary. [d1](../agents/core/iteration_budget.py) [d2](../agents/core/agent_runtime.py) [d3](../agents/core/orchestrator.py) |

| <a id="h444"></a>H444 | Reading documents into the conversation | update | Parțial | Reevaluat | The part this row says is worth copying is missing. A scanned (image-only) PDF extracts to empty or near-empty text, and file_read returns that as a normal successful read, with no coverage warning naming the page ranges that yielded no text. No size ceiling applies to a document: the whole file is parsed again on every page request, and max_bytes bounds only the text returned, where Hermes refuses documents over 50 MB. Paging is by byte offset, not a line offset/limit window. The wider format matrix (.ipynb, .xlsx, legacy Office, OpenDocument, .rtf, .epub) and Markdown conversion are not built; the row's rationale does not ask for them. ## automation

| ID | Capabilitate | Decizie inițială | Stare cod | Bază | Observație / restanță |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| <a id="h449"></a>H449 | Create, edit, pause, resume, trigger and delete a recurring scheduled job | copy | Parțial | Reevaluat | Lipsesc contextul izolat per job pentru model workdir și skills, agregarea între profile și noul grant schedule.create cerut de original (rădăcină protejată în afara scopului). Opțiunile fără runtime sunt refuzate, nu salvate inert. Nu există probă live pentru scheduler/canale sau autorare nativă mobilă; monitoarele depind de sursele configurate, nu instalează integrări. Cloud pins must match existing policy routing; changed local models require loaded context metadata, and LM Studio currently cannot resolve a changed model. Pinned jobs use deterministic compression and omit auxiliary embedding recall. The initial catalog intentionally excludes dedicated code-runner, delegation and dynamic plugin groups; their transitive restriction contracts remain unimplemented. [d1](../agents/core/autonomy/jobs.py) [d2](../agents/core/autonomy/jobs_blueprints.py) [d3](../agents/core/routers/jobs.py) |
| <a id="h450"></a>H450 | Say when in plain words (schedule syntax) | update | Parțial | Reevaluat | Four accepted schedule families are missing. (1) One-shot delays: \`in 30m\` / \`in 2 hours\` are refused, and there is no run-at/date trigger in the job store; every job is a cron. (2) ISO timestamps anchored to the configured timezone are missing. Worse, \`2026-10-01 09:00\`, \`tomorrow at 9\` and \`once at 9am\` are silently armed as a DAILY recurring cron \`0 9 \* \* \*\`, because _parse_time takes the HH:MM and a bare time falls through to daily (nl_schedule.py:96-97). (3) Compact intervals and bare durations as recurring schedules (\`every 2h\`, \`every 30m\`, \`2h\`) are missing. (4) There is no single repeat normaliser that coerces \`forever\|once\|1x\|N\`: options.repeat only accepts an integer or null. [d1](../agents/core/autonomy/nl_schedule.py) [d2](../agents/core/autonomy/jobs.py) [d3](../agents/core/commands.py) |
| <a id="h451"></a>H451 | See every attempt an autonomous run made, and whether the scheduler itself is alive | update | Parțial | Reevaluat | Rămân unele cerințe de sănătate/incident/continuitate din inventar, inclusiv context_from între joburi; verificarea trebuie făcută pe întregul rând. [d1](../agents/core/autonomy/jobs.py) [d2](../agents/core/routers/jobs.py) [d3](../tests/test_owner_jobs.py) |
| <a id="h452"></a>H452 | Repeated failures collapse into one acknowledgeable incident, and an unrunnable job pauses itself | copy | Parțial | Reevaluat | Rămân unele cerințe de sănătate/incident/continuitate din inventar, inclusiv context_from între joburi; verificarea trebuie făcută pe întregul rând. [d1](../agents/core/autonomy/jobs.py) [d2](../agents/core/routers/jobs.py) [d3](../tests/test_owner_jobs.py) |
| <a id="h453"></a>H453 | Let a cheap check decide whether the expensive model runs at all | copy | Parțial | Reevaluat | Unattended execution is not granted: each script firing and URL hop needs fresh approval, with live enforced signed mediation required for URL authoring. URL scope is credential-free public ASCII HTTP(S), at most five redirects, 30 seconds per hop and 256 KiB uncompressed UTF-8; private, credentialed, compressed or invalid-text observations are unsupported. Script snapshots remain bounded by the terminal cap (at most 50,000 bytes). Monitor mode excludes no_agent and treats wake JSON literally. Model workdir/tool contexts and monitor/URL authoring UI remain unsupported; script-only workdir and script/no_agent UI are implemented. No live production egress, scheduler/channel or full production-kernel proof. [d1](../agents/core/autonomy/jobs.py) [d2](../agents/core/autonomy/jobs_scripts.py) [d3](../agents/core/autonomy_coordinator.py) |

A job therefore always inherits the global \`llm.reasoning_effort\` / \`reasoning_effort_overrides\` read at agents/core/llm/hybrid_router.py:338, :351, :389, :418-419. The transport half already exists and is clamped: agents/core/llm/request_context.py:44-72; agents/core/llm/anthropic.py:81-86 with per-model overrides; agents/core/llm/gemini.py:134; agents/core/llm/xai.py:73; agents/core/llm/provider_request.py:46 and :54. Only the live chat path enters it (agents/web.py:1149, :1202). Work owed: - accept \`reasoning\` in validate_pins/validate_options against LADDER; - enter \`reasoning_scope(...)\` in \`_ask\` beside the other two scopes; - expose it on the CLI/HUD/doctor like the model and provider pins; - test it in tests/test_job_model_pins.py. (2) The bound can be expressed over only 8 of the ~18 tools the production server can register. CATALOG (agents/core/job_toolsets.py:7-11) covers echo/time, the five file tools and terminal_run. These belong to no group: web_search, web_extract, search_memory, session_search, execute_code, desktop_plan, desktop_run, operator_plan, osint_enrich, image_generate and (with the Media Director on) speak, registered at agents/core/autonomy_coordinator.py:442-774 and :778-860. A job that needs any of them must leave \`enabled_toolsets\` unset and run on the full default net. docs/superpowers/plans/2026-09-15-job-toolsets.md:44 names this residual itself ("does not claim ... arbitrary plugin/delegation/code-runner groups"), and doctor reports the upper bound, not the effective offers. (3) Minor: Hermes' middle precedence tier has no equivalent. That tier is a per-platform \`cron\` tools config that sets defaults for every scheduled job. Here, only the per-job list, the internal posture (\`llm.internal_actuation\`) and the agent's agents.yaml \`tools:\` patterns narrow an unattended run. The marker exists (Orchestrator.turn_lease's per-session lock), but /refine does not consult it, and a plain lock check would also refuse the command's own turn, which already holds that session's lease; the check has to exclude the caller's held lease. - The new or updated memories and skill proposals are not reported back into the chat. | <a id="h477"></a>H477 | Point an external client at Nerva (OpenAI-compatible chat, and capability discovery) | copy | Parțial | Reevaluat | Discovery half (the row's own scope), still owed: (1) a \`/v1/toolsets\` equivalent — \`GET /api/capabilities\` and \`GET /api/toolrpc/tools\` enumerate individual tools only; the toolset groups in \`agents/core/job_toolsets.py\` CATALOG (basic/files/terminal, with \`available\`) are reachable only inside admin-guarded \`GET /api/jobs/doctor\` (\`agents/core/autonomy/jobs_health.py:116\`) and are jobs-scoped, not the agent's platform toolsets with \`enabled\`/\`configured\`/expanded \`tools\`; (2) the API-surface half of \`/v1/capabilities\` — \`snapshot()\` (\`agents/core/observability/capability_registry.py:472\`) returns no \`features\`/\`endpoints\`/\`auth\`/\`session-header\`/idempotency/protocol-version block, so an external UI cannot feature-detect the server; \`/openapi.json\` partly covers the endpoint list but is not declared a stable client contract anywhere in docs/. Rolled-up children with no Nerva surface at all: \`.api-browser-control\` — no \`POST /v1/browser-control/register\`, no single-use TTL ticket, no controller websocket, no server-side controller capability allowlist (\`rg 'browser_control\|controller_id\|extension_control'\` over agents/ returns nothing; \`agents/core/browser_transport.py\` is SSRF resolver pinning for in-process Chromium); \`.api-platform-events\` — no normalization of platform-native reactions/edits (\`rg 'reaction\|message_edited\|platform_event'\` over agents/ returns no code, only food-diary prose in agents/frigga/\*.md; \`POST /api/channels/{channel_id}/inbound\` is message ingress). Partially satisfied children: \`.api-model-routing\` — \`ChatRequest\` (\`agents/web.py:973\`) is \`extra="forbid"\` and accepts only \`agent\`/\`reasoning\`/\`session_id\`, so per-request \`model\`/\`provider\` and \`model_routes\` alias precedence (including the 400-on-provider-conflict rule) do not exist; only the \`reasoning\` override is governed and live (\`agents/web.py:1149\`). \`.api-models\` — no \`/api/model/options\` provider-aware picker payload with refresh/cache-bust semantics; \`/api/models/local\` and \`/api/models/info\` (\`agents/core/routers/models_llm.py\`) are both behind \`admin_guard\`. \`.api-artifacts\` — \`agents/core/routers/artifact_store.py\` is bounded, quota'd and guarded, but performs no server-side SHA-256 digest validation and binds no artifact to the scope that created it. Out of scope by the row's own rationale, not gaps: \`POST /v1/chat/completions\` (explicitly refused) and \`POST /v1/responses\` (same chat-façade family; any façade must re-enter the governed \`POST /chat\` path at \`agents/web.py:1120\`). Namespace note for the build: \`GET /v1\` is already the legacy HUD HTML (\`agents/web.py:1082\`), so a \`/v1/\*\` API prefix has to be reconciled with it or the discovery surface stays on \`/api/\*\`. | <a id="h478"></a>H478 | Bot-to-bot messaging between machines | keep | Parțial | Reevaluat | Nothing sends outbound. Peer records store no endpoint, and no code signs a task and POSTs it to another machine's /api/a2a/task, so this box has no \`peer dm\` equivalent. An approved inbound task has no execution path: decide only changes the status, no turn runs and no reply reaches the sender. The accepted async handle is missing: there is no start/status/stop for an approved remote turn and no peer-authenticated status/result poll. Without them the sender cannot collect a result without holding a connection open. The row depends on H476 (Runs API), which is itself partial. Any execution must stay behind owner approval and node.dispatch/kernel mediation, and no peer-side grant may skip the pending inbox.
