| <a id="h479"></a>H479 | Named inbound webhook routes that wake the agent — or deliver without one | update | Parțial | Audit 07.09 | Reevaluare pe cod; cerințele și lipsurile sunt în rândul original.  |
| <a id="h480"></a>H480 | Send a message to any configured channel from a script, with no model call | copy | Parțial | Reevaluat | Atașamentele \`MEDIA:\<path\>\` nu sunt purtate: o rută care citește căi locale și le trimite pe un canal e egress de fișiere și trebuie să ajungă în spatele Action Kernel-ului, cu un director-sursă delimitat, înainte să existe. Cerința structurală „fără gateway pornit” rămâne o divergență documentată: \`nerva send\` trece prin hub ca să existe IntentLog și rate-limit. Corpurile peste 4.000 de caractere sunt refuzate, nu fragmentate (Hermes fragmentează per platformă). Slack și Discord rămân reply-only (restul H018); email-ul e exclus deliberat. Nu există adresare \`platform:chat_id\[:thread\]\` / \`platform:#canal\` ca la Hermes: \`--channel telegram\` înseamnă întotdeauna chat-ul owner-ului. Precedența diverge deliberat: la Hermes argumentul câștigă în fața \`--file\`, aici ambele deodată e eroare de utilizare. Erorile nu au prefixul \`hermes send:\`. \`JobRunner._send\` își păstrează propria rezolvare a destinației (lot-ul jobs). Fără probă live pe un canal real din această sesiune: verificarea a fost prin hub fals și prin ChannelManager-ul real cu adaptoare false. [d1](../agents/core/channels/outbound.py) [d2](../agents/core/routers/integrations.py) [d3](../agents/cli/nerva.py) |

## security

| ID | Capabilitate | Decizie inițială | Stare cod | Bază | Observație / restanță |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| <a id="h481"></a>H481 | Refuse a catastrophic shell command no matter who approved it (hardline floor + risky-pattern tier + de-obfuscation) | update | Parțial | Reevaluat | \*\*The middle tier does not exist.\*\* The row asks for three stacked layers; this is the first, with its matching repaired (and repaired again in PR #1177). There is still no ~40-rule severity/reversibility-tagged risky-pattern catalogue feeding the approval card's description, so \`rm -rf ~/projects\` is neither hardline nor pattern-classified — re-checked 2026-09-19 on 494a13b2: \`hardline_match("rm -rf ~/projects")\` and \`hardline_match(\['rm','-rf','/home/x/projects'\])\` both return None, and the module carries no severity/reversibility/catalogue vocabulary at all — and the owner's card still says "run this argv" rather than "recursive delete", which the row's rationale names as the point. No user-editable \`approvals.deny\` fnmatch list at hardline level (no \`fnmatch\`/\`deny\` in the module). No parser limits that themselves hardline-block and save an oversized payload to a file for manual review (128k chars / 4096 separator-free / 25k segments); \`MAX_ARG_CHARS = 4000\` is argv validation, not a refusal. \*\*Variants Hermes produces and this still does not:\*\* NFKC normalisation, \`$HOME\`/\`$HERMES_HOME\` folding, grep-pattern span masking, and \`python -c\` / \`perl -e\` / \`node -e\` payload extraction — the last left alone on purpose, since \`jobs_scripts.snapshot_script\` legitimately builds \`\[sys.executable, '-I', '-c', source\]\`. \`$()\`/backtick substitution is now extracted in ONE place only: #1177's \`_substituted\` narrows a heredoc BODY line to its substitutions, so \`cat \<\<EOF\` / \`$(rm -rf /)\` / \`EOF\` refuses; an ordinary line is still not expanded, so \`echo $(rm -rf /)\` and a backtick \`rm -rf /\` inside an assignment return None (a \`$(mkfs.ext4 …)\` is caught only through \`_CMD\`'s \`(\` anchor). The row's own examples \`$(printf mkfs).ext4 /dev/sda\` and \`echo bWtmcw==\|base64 -d\|sh\` still return None. \*\*Gaps this module knows it still has, listed honestly rather than as a complete set:\*\* nesting deeper than \`_MAX_SHELL_DEPTH\` (depth-3 \`sh -c "sh -c 'sh -c …'"\` returns None, pinned by test); a quote at a word BOUNDARY (\`'mkfs.ext4' /dev/sda\`) rather than inside a word; \`powershell -EncodedCommand\` base64; a \`cmd /c\` payload split across several argv items; an obfuscated shell HEAD in argv form (\`\['s""h','-c',…\]\`), because the de-obfuscation pass runs after payload extraction and its output is not fed back through it; and, since #1177, two in the false-refusal direction — a \`case\` label with a leading \`(\` (\`(shutdown) echo bye ;;\`) is still read as a command, and a \`case\` nested inside another is not modelled: its inner first label IS stripped (\`case $1 in\\na) case $2 in\\nreboot) echo r ;;\\nesac ;;\\nesac\` went power_cycle→None), so the docstring's 'not handled' is about the region boundary, not a guaranteed refusal. \*\*Refusals #1177 cleared in the dangerous direction — closed by this slice (H481, 2026-09-22).\*\* The \`case\`-label strip fires only inside a real command-position \`case WORD in … esac\` region now, so \`(echo case in; reboot); echo esac\` and the \`$IFS\`-spliced \`(echo case in; mkfs.ext4$IFS/dev/sda); echo esac\` / \`wipefs$IFS-a$IFS/dev/sda\` refuse again; and \`_heredoc_feeds_a_shell\` now also sees a body a shell consumes AFTER the redirection through a pipe (\`cat \<\<EOF \| sh\`, \`cat \<\<EOF \| bash\`, \`cat \<\<EOF \| grep … \| sh\`) or via a file a shell later runs (\`cat \<\<'EOF' \>x.sh; sh x.sh\`), so those refuse — and, after the review round, so do \`(sh x.sh)\`, \`{ sh x.sh; }\`, \`if true; then sh x.sh; fi\`, \`cat x.sh \| sh\`, \`sh -c '. x.sh'\`, \`. x.sh\`, \`source x.sh\`, \`tee x.sh \<\<EOF; sh x.sh\`, a quoted \`\> "x.sh"\` target, \`sh /dev/stdin \<\<EOF\`, \`bash -c sh \<\<EOF\` and \`cat \<\<EOF \| eval "$(cat)"\`, each verified to run the body. Pinned in tests/test_local_transport.py, reproduced against /bin/sh, /bin/dash and /bin/bash first, and screened by a 671,995-case differential fuzz vs origin/main whose every difference is explained by the two changed mechanisms (numbers in the summary; the earlier \`refusals_cleared = 0\` claim is withdrawn). Still open in this direction, listed in \`_detection_variants\` and pinned as misses: a heredoc body written to a file run by a bare path (\`… \>x.sh; ./x.sh\`), by a variable-carried name (\`for f in x.sh; do sh $f; done\`) or by the environment (\`BASH_ENV=x.sh bash -c :\`); a heredoc opened inside a command substitution (\`eval "$(cat \<\<EOF …)"\`, read as quoted text); \`xargs -I{} sh -c '{}'\` (each line becomes the command line); and a \`;\` inside a \`( )\` group of a pipe stage (\`cat \<\<EOF \| (cd /tmp; sh)\`). \*\*Two pre-existing false positives are unchanged\*\* (\`git commit -m "fix;mkfs.ext4 handling"\` → mkfs; \`grep -r "(shutdown" docs/\` → power_cycle), and \`_PATH_PREFIX\` added a third class no approval can lift: a path to a script whose basename starts with a hardline name — \`./mkfs.sh\` (mkfs), \`./reboot.sh\`, \`./scripts/shutdown.sh --dry-run\`, \`/opt/app/bin/halt\` (power_cycle) — all None on 1c5054ea, all refused on 494a13b2 (bare \`reboot.sh\` already refused before; the prefix extends it to every path spelling). This slice left that (c) class unclosed on purpose: any name- or path-only rule that cleared \`./mkfs.sh\` or \`/opt/app/bin/halt\` would also clear a TRUE refusal — \`./mkfs.sh\` is structurally \`mkfs.\<suffix\>\` exactly like the real \`/sbin/mkfs.ext4\`, and \`/opt/app/bin/halt\` is \`\<path\>/\<hardline-name\>\` exactly like a copied \`/tmp/mkfs.ext4 /dev/sda\` — so it needs approval-context (is the target a workspace script or a real system binary) the pure static floor does not have. \*\*And a measured cost, re-measured after #1177 removed the breadth cap\*\* (\`_MAX_SHELL_EXPANSIONS\` and the \`MAX_ARG_CHARS\` re-expansion guard are gone; every level-1 payload is unwrapped again): on a maximal 64x4000-character argv the pass is roughly unchanged (79.6 ms on the pre-#1177 module vs 82.3 ms after, same machine, best of 5), but a command made of 2,000 nested \`sh -c "sh -c 'echo ok'"\` segments (57k chars) goes from 196 ms to 380 ms — synchronous, on the event loop, before every exec. The docstring's linear-in-input argument holds (each level's payloads are disjoint substrings); an independent re-run on another machine saw 79.0→80.6 ms and 125→151 ms, so the constant grows for nested input but the factor is machine-dependent and no test pins either number. This slice's first pass claimed no measurable cost on that maximal 64x4000-character argv (78.7 → 78.4 ms); the review showed the claim held only for input with no \`case\` word and never exercised the redirect-target branch, which was quadratic. Re-measured after the review round (best of 3, same machine, origin/main → this branch): the maximal 64x4000 argv 62.0 → 58.6 ms; the same argv filled with case/in prose 18,207 → 105 ms (main's own \`\\bcase\\b.\*?\\besac\\b\` was super-linear there too); 4000 chars of \`case x in\` 21.6 → 6.5 ms; eight 4000-char argv items of prose 316 → 14 ms; 400 \`cat \<\<EOF \| sh\` blocks (12 KB) 39.8 → 64.9 ms; 400 \`cat \<\<E \>x\` blocks in 4000 chars 25 → 39 ms (2,471 ms on the first pass); 1,600 such blocks (33.6 KB) 132 → 201 ms (97.5 s on the first pass); 2,000 nested \`sh -c\` segments 146 → 148 ms; 300 real \`case\` statements 57 → 60 ms. The heredoc-with-target shapes cost about 1.5x main, linear, because the statement lines after a heredoc are tokenised once and shared; nothing else pays. Six test items pin wall-clock bounds (0.25–2 s) two orders of magnitude above these; no test pins a ratio. \*\*Adversarial review of the original slice found four more defects, all fixed and each reproduced first\*\* against real /bin/sh, /bin/dash and /bin/bash: (1) \`_shell_c_payloads\` demanded the word after the wrappers BE the shell, so \`env FOO=1 sh -c 'mkfs.ext4 /dev/sda'\` — and \`sudo -u root sh -c …\`, \`nice -n 10 sh -c …\` — skipped the whole \`sh -c\` screening while deleting \`FOO=1 \` made the same string \`hardline_denied:mkfs\`; \`_CMD\` had been widened for that assignment prefix and the unwrapper had not. (2) \`_unquoted_lines\` read a backslash-newline as a statement separator when it is a line CONTINUATION, so \`mk\\\` + newline + \`fs.ext4 /dev/sda\`, which all three shells splice into \`mkfs.ext4\`, was seen as two harmless fragments. (3) every heredoc body line became a command position, so \`cat \> x.yaml \<\<'EOF'\` / \`shutdown: graceful\` / \`EOF\` was refused as \`power_cycle\`; body lines were joined as data in the flat text. \*\*#1177's second review showed that fix was incomplete in three ways, all closed there:\*\* \`segments\` still tokenised every body line, so \`cat \> README.md \<\<'EOF'\` / \`rm -rf / will destroy the box\` / \`EOF\` stayed a \`recursive_root_removal\` refusal; the rule had no exception for a heredoc a SHELL consumes, so \`bash \<\<EOF\` / \`mkfs.ext4 /dev/sda\` / \`EOF\` returned None (a bypass the heredoc work itself opened); and any \`\<\<\` — inside quotes, inside \`$(( ))\`, a \`\<\<\<\` here-string, or with a delimiter that never returns — opened a heredoc and switched the newline screening off for every following line. (4) \`{\` anchored bare, so \`jq '{reboot: .needs_reboot}'\` was a \`power_cycle\` refusal; POSIX requires whitespace after \`{\` for a brace group, and \`{ mkfs.ext4 /dev/sda; }\` is still refused. Each fix is red-proofed on its own. [d1](../agents/core/environments/terminal_contract.py) [d2](../tests/test_local_transport.py) |

| <a id="h495"></a>H495 | Never let a credential reach a log, a transcript, an approval card or an export | update | Parțial | Reevaluat | This is one of the row's seven paths. Hermes applies \`redact.py\` to logs, verbose output, tool output, approval prompts, gateway messages, transcripts and memory; this slice covers LOGS. Still owed, and the rationale calls them the part that matters most: the hash-chained audit DB (a \`cat .env\` from \`terminal_run\` still writes a live credential into an append-only log that by design cannot be edited), the decision-inbox approval card the owner reads on a phone, task payloads, terminal output, and the observability exports — which Hermes scrubs unconditionally with no knob, returning \`\[redaction-unavailable\]\` rather than raw text. Also absent: the second mask mode (a syntactically INVALID \`«redacted:ghp_…»\` sentinel for file reads, so an agent cannot write a masked-but-plausible key back and silently kill the credential), \`code_file=True\` sparing source constants, \`_mask_control_split_tokens()\` for tokens smuggled across ANSI/zero-width characters, and the shared \`.env\` basename list. The two silent-degradation paths named at review are now CLOSED, after the SAST gate forced the question: a handler whose \`addFilter\` fails is counted and published through \`uncovered_handler_count()\` and a warning naming how many handlers are writing unredacted, and a \`loggerDict\` walk that raises warns that coverage fell back to root-only. The one silent swallow left is the filter's own last-ditch blank (\`# nosec B110\`), where a \`logging.Filter\` that raises would take down the caller's log call and the scanner has already failed. \`install_log_redaction()\` remains exported although the \`everywhere\` variant is what production uses. \*\*A later slice's full-suite run surfaced one more thing about this control, recorded here rather than fixed:\*\* the scanner's \`high_entropy_secret\` pattern matches an ordinary filesystem path. Under \`-n auto --dist loadfile\` a warning naming a pytest tmp file arrived as \`truncated: \[REDACTED:high_entropy_secret\].md\`, which is what a real operator would see when a log line names a file with a long random-looking segment — a debuggability cost the module docstring already warns about in general terms, now with a concrete case. Separately, \`setup_logging()\` installs the filter on every handler of every logger and nothing took it back off, so every later test on the same xdist worker ran under a live redactor. The installer is PRODUCTION code, not the tests: \`agents/run.py\` calls \`setup_logging()\` at module level, so importing it installs the filter during pytest COLLECTION — before any fixture runs — and eight test files import it; \`agents/web.py\` calls it from the FastAPI lifespan, so any test that drives the app installs it again; five test files call it directly on top of that. There is no list of leaking files to fix. The cleanup therefore lives in \`tests/conftest.py\` as an autouse fixture that strips unconditionally BEFORE each test as well as after — a snapshot-and-restore fixture, which is what the two neighbouring isolation fixtures do and what this was first written as, treats a collection-time install as pre-existing and preserves it for the life of the worker. That version went green locally and RED in CI on exactly the test it was meant to fix. The first attempt at it matched by \`isinstance\` and silently did nothing: \`agents/\` is on \`sys.path\`, so \`core.security.log_redaction\` and \`agents.core.security.log_redaction\` are two module objects over this one file with two distinct \`SecretRedactionFilter\` classes, and the root handler was carrying the other one. The match is by class name now, and four paired tests pin both halves — including the alternate import path, which is the case the first attempt missed. [d1](../agents/core/security/log_redaction.py) [d2](../agents/core/log.py) [d3](../tests/test_log_redaction.py) |

| <a id="h497"></a>H497 | Let a stranger reach the assistant only after the owner says so | update | Parțial | Reevaluat | \*\*The row names four hardening gaps; (a) is closed, (c) is closed for the deeplink path, (b) and (d) are open.\*\* (c) \*revocation reaching live adapters\*: \`revoke_deeplinks()\` and \`unpair()\` now act on the one object the Telegram adapter redeems from and the gateway consults on every message, so there is no second copy for a link or an approval to survive in — pinned by \`test_revoking_on_the_shared_store_reaches_the_live_telegram_adapter\`. Slack and Discord have no deeplink path and gate through the same gateway instance. Still outstanding, from the row itself: (b) \*no lockout after N failed attempts\* — the row wants a 1 h lockout after 5 failed approvals, checked BEFORE the pending lookup so a code already in flight cannot be brute-forced past it; there is still only a rate limit and an in-memory guess budget, both of which reset on restart. (d) \*no admin-vs-user command split on channels\*, so any paired sender gets every command; the row wants a split with an always-allowed floor of \`{help, whoami}\`. \*\*Unaddressed from the Hermes column:\*\* pairing-code GENERATION (8 characters from a 32-char unambiguous alphabet via \`secrets.choice\`) and the code's 1 h TTL — the code here never expires; "max 3 pending per platform" (the store bounds 200 globally); "1 request per user per 10 min" (it is 5); \`unauthorized_dm_behavior\` treating inbox-shaped platforms differently from chat-shaped ones; and the ordered authorization union (allow-all flag → pairing approvals → per-platform allowlist → global allowlist → global allow-all → default DENY), which is still an ad-hoc sequence in \`is_allowed\`/\`gate_inbound\`. \*\*Two honest limits of the second slice.\*\* The store itself still has no defence against a second instance — the guarantee is one instance per process by construction, and the premise test says so in words. And an install that had relocated its data root (\`JARVIS_HOME\`) and paired senders through the HUD kept them in \`\<cwd\>/memory_logs/sender_pairing.json\`; the store now opens under the data root, so those senders are held once more until re-approved (the owner's allowlisted ids never lived in that file and are unaffected). \*\*And the limit carried from the first slice.\*\* Stretching is a work factor, not a wall: a four-digit code goes from milliseconds to minutes, an eight-character mixed-alphabet code becomes infeasible, and \*\*nothing enforces a floor on what the owner types\*\* — \`PairingCodeBody\` caps the length at 128 and sets no minimum — while generating the code for them, the row's own prescription, is not implemented. [d1](../agents/core/channels/pairing.py) [d2](../agents/core/channels/telegram.py) [d3](../agents/web.py) |

| <a id="h501"></a>H501 | Warn about a dangerous deployment posture before it becomes an incident | update | Parțial | Reevaluat | Add three read-only, non-blocking host checks, each logged once at startup and reported in both run_doctor and /api/security/posture. (1) The process runs as root (euid 0), or elevated on Windows, on any host, including headless. (2) sshd accepts passwords: parse /etc/ssh/sshd_config and its Include'd sshd_config.d fragments with sshd's own precedence, treating absence as the default 'yes'. (3) Running in a container (/.dockerenv, /run/.containerenv or cgroup markers) while data_root() is not on a volume or bind mount in /proc/self/mountinfo, which means memory, vault and keys vanish on restart. [d1](../agents/core/boot_guards.py) [d2](../agents/core/routers/security.py) [d3](../scripts/doctor.py) |
| <a id="h502"></a>H502 | Refuse an MCP server entry that is a backdoor wearing an MCP costume, and never hand one the owner's keys | copy | Parțial | Reevaluat | \*\*The documented per-server \`env\` migration path does not exist as a reachable surface.\*\* \`MCPServerConfig\` has no \`env\` field (\`routers/mcp.py:128-140\`, no \`model_config\`), \`POST /api/admin/mcp\` drops the key (pydantic's default \`extra="ignore"\`), and neither \`to_config\` (\`client.py:798-812\`) nor \`load_from_config\` (\`:814-849\`) writes or reads one — the module docstring says as much at \`client.py:41-45\`. The only remedy an owner can actually reach is the \*\*host-wide\*\* \`JARVIS_MCP_STDIO_ALLOWED_ENV\` (\`:92-98\`, "Host-wide, not per-server"), which is a strictly weaker grant than the per-server one the docs describe: an owner whose forge server needs \`GITHUB_TOKEN\` must allow it for every stdio server on the host. Whether to ship on that or build the per-server surface first is a product decision, written up as \`docs/OWNER_TASKS.md\` §P28.3 with both options. \*\*Related and also owner-level:\*\* \`XAUTHORITY\` and \`DBUS_SESSION_BUS_ADDRESS\` are both on \`STDIO_ENV_ALLOWLIST\` (\`client.py:169\`), and today one is listed-but-silently-dropped (\`_SECRET_LIKE\` at \`:174-176\` matches the \`AUTH\` in \`XAUTHORITY\`) while the other is listed-and-handed-over — they should be consistent in one direction or the other. \*\*And the honest ceiling, stated wherever the guarantee is:\*\* this is \*\*defence in depth, not a security boundary\*\*, and since #1179 the module states that ceiling by TWO routes rather than one (\`client.py:17-36\`). The child is same-UID with no namespace, so a server that can run a subprocess reads the withheld variables straight out of \`/proc/\<ppid\>/environ\`; and the baseline narrows the \*\*filesystem\*\* not at all — \`cwd\` defaults to the hub's own working directory, so \`open(".env")\` hands back exactly the keys that were withheld, which \`cwd_secret_files()\` now says out loud at connect (WARNING, filenames only, \`client.py:565-571\`) rather than leaving the owner to infer a protection from the withheld count alone. What the baseline buys is that a \*careless\* or \*curious\* server does not get them by accident, and that an owner's \`JARVIS_\*\` settings are not ambient in a third party's process. It does not stop an adversary, and the row says so rather than implying otherwise; closing the filesystem route as well as the \`/proc\` one needs real process isolation (separate UID, namespace or container — \`client.py:33-36\`), which is not shipped. \*\*Unaddressed from the row:\*\* there is no refusal of an MCP entry that is a backdoor wearing an MCP costume — no command allow-listing, no signature or provenance check on the spawned binary (the docstring itself calls the screener "still-unshipped", \`client.py:34\`), and \`POST /api/admin/mcp\` still stores \`req.command\` verbatim after checking only transport, trust tier, tool-filter shape and bounds, and duplicate name (\`routers/mcp.py:142-192\`, \`:184\`); the only shape check is \`_command_argv()\`'s shell-metacharacter/NUL refusal at connect (\`client.py:519-529\`), which is not a screener. #1179 changed none of this — it explicitly chose disclosure over refusal. This slice hardens what the child \*receives\*; the row also asks who the child is allowed to \*be\*. [d1](../agents/core/mcp/client.py) [d2](../agents/core/routers/mcp.py) [d3](../agents/core/mcp/worldview_write.py) |
| <a id="h503"></a>H503 | Unpack an archive from another machine without letting it write outside the destination | update | Echivalent | Reevaluat | archive_safe is the only module in Nerva that reads an archive. Backup verify and restore unpack through extract_tar_path (agents/core/archive_safe.py:573; agents/core/backup.py:558, agents/core/backup.py:627). Marketplace install_from_zip unpacks through extract_zip_bytes (agents/core/archive_safe.py:637; agents/core/skills/marketplace.py:795). The old backup._safe_extract and the marketplace _safe_targets+extractall pass are gone. An AST scan (tests/test_archive_safe.py:702, with probes at tests/test_archive_safe.py:688) fails when any other product module imports tarfile or zipfile outside a write-only allowlist, opens an archive for reading or calls an extract-family method. normalize_member (agents/core/archive_safe.py:122) folds backslash to '/' first. It then refuses empty, NUL, POSIX-absolute, Windows-absolute, UNC and drive-lettered names, and any '..' or empty part. Only regular files and directories are written. Symlink, hardlink, device, fifo and sparse tar members raise (agents/core/archive_safe.py:504), and so do symlink, device, fifo, socket and encrypted zip entries (agents/core/archive_safe.py:596). _Planner (agents/core/archive_safe.py:182) validates every member before the first write: name, type, in-archive conflicts and duplicates, member count and declared bytes (agents/core/archive_safe.py:257). It also checks the destination's existing content (agents/core/archive_safe.py:286): a link on the way to a member is refused, and so is a directory where a file goes (tests/test_archive_safe.py:365, tests/test_backup.py:471). The pre-scan reads the stream to its end (agents/core/archive_safe.py:522), so a bad gzip CRC, trailing data or a corrupt header part-way through is a refusal, not a shortened tree (tests/test_archive_safe.py:482, tests/test_archive_safe.py:517). Bytes are counted again while streaming (agents/core/archive_safe.py:389). The byte budget is clamped to free space plus the blocks of the single-link files the archive replaces (agents/core/archive_safe.py:314), so an in-place --force restore fits (tests/test_backup.py:443). PAX and GNU long-name header payloads are capped before tarfile reads them, 1 MiB each and 256 MiB in total (agents/core/archive_safe.py:461, agents/core/archive_safe.py:483; tests/test_backup.py:515). A link or hardlinked name at a member's path is unlinked and the file is created O_EXCL\|O_NOFOLLOW, never written through (agents/core/archive_safe.py:373; tests/test_archive_safe.py:323, tests/test_archive_safe.py:349). Backups take their caps from JARVIS_BACKUP_MAX_MEMBERS and JARVIS_BACKUP_MAX_BYTES (agents/core/backup.py:85); skill packages use SKILL_PACKAGE_LIMITS (agents/core/skills/marketplace.py:41). On the producing side, create_backup writes both the encrypted and the plaintext branch through atomic_write, a sibling temp file that is fsynced and then os.replace'd (agents/core/archive_safe.py:654; agents/core/backup.py:375, agents/core/backup.py:380). Archives are GNU-format tar (agents/core/backup.py:434). The export walk skips symlinks (agents/core/archive_safe.py:715; agents/core/backup.py:365). It is also used by publish_skill (agents/core/skills/marketplace.py:371), which has its own test (tests/test_marketplace_governance_hf12_12.py:217). Every other name, hardlinked ones included, is archived as its own regular member (agents/core/backup.py:394, agents/core/backup.py:418; tests/test_backup.py:388). verify and restore compare the manifest's file_count with what the archive held (agents/core/backup.py:585; tests/test_backup.py:430). install_from_zip stages inside skills_dir, runs the signature gate there, and only then renames the tree into place (agents/core/skills/marketplace.py:834, agents/core/skills/marketplace.py:844). A non-zip upload is a 400 refusal (tests/test_marketplace_governance_hf12_12.py:192). Staging left by a killed install is swept once it is an hour old (agents/core/skills/marketplace.py:752; tests/test_marketplace_governance_hf12_12.py:201). Restore stays an operator CLI action. It prints 'partial restore' when a failure came after the first write (agents/core/backup.py:676) and 'nothing restored' only for a pre-scan refusal (agents/core/backup.py:682). tests/test_backup.py:606 pins that no product module, script or subprocess caller reaches it. The pre-scan also refuses a component longer than the destination's NAME_MAX or a path past PATH_MAX, which the existing-path walk cannot see under a new directory (agents/core/archive_safe.py:251; tests/test_archive_safe.py:726), and a write the destination refuses mid-restore (permissions, a full disk) is a named ArchiveRejected marked partial, never a traceback (agents/core/archive_safe.py:445; tests/test_archive_safe.py:738). A restore leaves the archive's manifest in the target; create_backup never archives a top-level backup_manifest.json from the data root (agents/core/backup.py:442), so a backup of a restored root carries one manifest and verifies, restores and serves the forget purge's verified pre-snapshot (tests/test_backup.py:636, tests/test_backup.py:675). A backup of a restored root made before this change carries that name twice; verify and restore accept exactly that one repeat, last wins as tarfile resolved it, and count the dropped copy against the manifest's file_count (agents/core/backup.py:559, agents/core/backup.py:602; tests/test_backup.py:651). The repeat is bounded: the dropped copy still counts against the member and byte caps, and a third copy or any other duplicate is refused (agents/core/archive_safe.py:220, agents/core/archive_safe.py:223; tests/test_archive_safe.py:768, tests/test_archive_safe.py:775, tests/test_archive_safe.py:790). Known limit: backups made on main from a data root holding a symlink or hardlink fail verify and restore by design. [d1](../agents/core/archive_safe.py) [d2](../agents/core/backup.py) [d3](../agents/core/skills/marketplace.py) |
| <a id="h504"></a>H504 | Fail loudly when TLS trust is misconfigured instead of silently not verifying | update | Parțial | Reevaluat | 1. Add boot-time validation, run from the lifespan beside enforce_boot_posture, of every set CA variable (JARVIS_CA_BUNDLE, SSL_CERT_FILE, SSL_CERT_DIR, REQUESTS_CA_BUNDLE, CURL_CA_BUNDLE) and of certifi. Each must exist, be a file (or a directory for SSL_CERT_DIR), load into an SSLContext and yield at least one certificate. The error must name the variable and give a copy-pasteable repair command. 2. Route every LLM backend client through the same trust resolver, so JARVIS_CA_BUNDLE applies to model egress and a bad SSL_CERT_FILE cannot surface as an unnamed FileNotFoundError. 3. Add an explicit per-target (per-provider or per-base-URL) verify resolver. Its opt-in 'off' position logs a loud WARNING naming the base URL whenever a client is built with it. A bad CA path never turns into verify=False. [d1](../agents/core/http_client.py) [d2](../agents/core/llm/egress.py) [d3](../agents/core/llm/anthropic.py) |
| <a id="h505"></a>H505 | Keep the agent's file tools out of credential stores and system files | keep | Echivalent | Audit 07.09 | Preluat din auditul 07.09; nereevaluat în această livrare.  |
| <a id="h506"></a>H506 | Treat files that steer future runs as a distinct always-ask class, on the way in and on the way out | copy | Parțial | Reevaluat | \*\*The read half now covers two of the three Nerva loaders and none of the external ones — the row stays partial.\*\* H506 requires the same file class to be scanned at context-load time with a positive match replaced by \`\[BLOCKED: … Content not loaded.\]\`. What holds: SOUL.md / SOUL.local.md through \`Agent._scan_soul_body\` (H387's evidence, not this row's — per-line quarantine over the union of raw and normalised copies, cross-line wraps, whole-body drop above the ratio; the U+200B and U+0007 spellings after 'now' are pinned in this row's own test file), and HEARTBEAT.md / HEARTBEAT.local.md through \`scan_heartbeat_config\`, entry refused rather than line-stubbed. \*\*Precisely what is not covered, stated rather than smoothed over:\*\* the Markdown body after the front-matter is discarded by \`_parse_heartbeat\` and reaches no model, log or scheduler, so it is not scanned — \`test_the_prose_body_is_not_loaded_and_therefore_not_scanned\` is the tripwire a loader that starts reading it must flip; no heartbeat text is model input today — the checklist strings are keyword-routed to skills with constant arguments and echoed into a logged run summary — so the scan guards the mapping the loader hands to the agent, not a live prompt path; the keyword scanner is literal after normalisation, so a Cyrillic homoglyph ('Іgnore'), a combining overlay, markdown emphasis inside a phrase ('Ignore _all_ previous') and a phrase split across two checklist items still scan clean — a confusables fold is a separate \`quarantine.py\` slice, and the homoglyph is pinned as a tripwire in this row's test file; and the SOUL cross-line path (\`_wrapped_injection_hits\`) builds its own normalised copy inside \`agent.py\`, so a blank-instead-of-space spelling that ALSO wraps across lines is not covered there (agent.py is untouched here; it is H387's evidence, being re-evaluated by a sibling stream). \*\*Adjacent, pre-existing, NOT fixed:\*\* the orchestrator's \`load_from_config\` overwrites every shipped HEARTBEAT.md cron+checklist with the agents.yaml interval (all twelve shipped agents carry one), so at runtime \`Agent.run_heartbeat\` finds no checklist and returns None for the shipped roster — the loader semantics this row describes are what the code does, not what the shipped agents experience (recorded as a separate task); \`load_all\` keys the config by the front-matter's own \`agent\` field, so a clean \`HEARTBEAT.local.md\` in one agent's directory that says \`agent: jarvis\` replaces Jarvis's schedule (a refused file cannot — the verdict is keyed by directory); and \`enabled: false\` is parsed and never honoured. Still open on the read side: AGENTS.md / CLAUDE.md / GEMINI.md / \`.cursorrules\` / \`\*.mdc\` are read by the external harnesses rather than by Nerva, so 'the same file class on the way in' is not met for them. The write-side deviations stand as before: the row's \*"fails closed on a config read error"\* clause has no analogue because the roster is a hard-coded frozenset; \`allow_session=False\` holds only incidentally through the gated toolrpc \`autonomy_level="ask"\` intake, with nothing pinning it; and the class covers \`file_write\`/\`file_delete\` only — the terminal contract, the skill importer and the desktop operator are outside it. Unverified, stated as such: whether the Telegram decision card and the HUD decision inbox render the write-side notice verbatim, and whether any HUD renders the \`blocked\` list (the API carries it; \`frontend/src/gap.tsx\` and \`agents/web/static/systems.js\` read \`heartbeats\` only). \`_mutate\`'s floor remains lifted by a caller-supplied \`approved=True\` by design. [d1](../agents/core/file_tools.py) [d2](../agents/core/tool_rpc.py) [d3](../tests/test_file_tools_instruction_floor.py) |

It must cover unsafe deserialization (pickle/dill, yaml.load without SafeLoader, torch.load without weights_only), command injection (os.system, shell=True, child_process.exec, Go exec.Command("sh")), code injection (eval, new Function), XSS sinks, crypto footguns (ECB, createCipher without IV, disabled TLS verification), XXE, missing SRI and \`${{ github.event.\* }}\` inside workflow \`run:\`. \`model.eval()\` must not match. Its findings must be attached to the file_write approval card and write result and to skill install/generation results, with a kill switch. It must never block. | <a id="h510"></a>H510 | Stop a web page or a rebound DNS name from driving the local API | update | Parțial | Reevaluat | The Host guard exempts _PROBE_PATHS (agents/web.py:683, applied at :740-741), so /healthz, /readyz and /metrics answer a rebound name: live-verified, \`Host: evil.test\` returns 200 on /healthz and /metrics and reaches the route on /readyz, while every real API route returns 400. Because DNS rebinding makes the browser treat those responses as same-origin, an attacker page still reads the full Prometheus body (route templates, request counters, latency buckets) and the /readyz body naming the configured LLM backend and the agent/channel counts. The code names this residual itself at agents/web.py:716-718 ("a rebound page can therefore still read the golden-signal counters"). Close it by narrowing the exemption rather than removing it — the operability reason for it is sound. Suggested shape: keep /healthz and /readyz exempt but strip /readyz's detail to a bare ready/not-ready when the Host is not accepted, and require an accepted Host for /metrics unless the request is unambiguously non-browser (no Origin and no Sec-Fetch-Site, or an explicit JARVIS_METRICS_ALLOW_ANY_HOST opt-in for a monitor that scrapes by an unlisted name). Needs a test alongside test_healthz_from_any_host_is_exempt asserting that a refused Host cannot read the counter body. [d1](../agents/core/host_policy.py) [d2](../agents/web.py) [d3](../agents/core/proxy_trust.py) |

Emit them from _admin_guard and _user_guard: failures always; successes deduplicated per token hash and client so the chain is not flooded. Also emit from TokenStore issue/rotate/revoke_all, which covers the CLI. Each event records scope, client host, reason and a token-hash prefix. A fixed redacted-field set (token, x-admin-token, x-user-token, authorization, cookie, code, state, ticket) is dropped before serialisation. Writes are best-effort: an audit failure must never change the allow/401/403 decision. The rotation route should switch to the new type. Tests must cover all of this. | <a id="h515"></a>H515 | Generate an image from a prompt (and edit an existing image) | copy | Parțial | Reevaluat | The real ComfyUI path does not yet use the existing LLM unload/restore orchestrator. Frozen media.generate/file.write action-kind requirements need explicit architecture adjudication; current local tool.rpc and cloud plugin.egress mediation are verified but are not silently equated to that requirement. Local reference blending is pixel composition and upscale is bicubic resampling. The accepted adaptation requires one cloud seam, not eight-provider fan-out. Ambiguous provider attempts never retry automatically; catalog opt-out is respected. No live ComfyUI, billed provider or native-device validation is claimed. This acknowledgment foundation is not a VRAM swap: canonical aliases, production backend/controller identity, atomic per-generation leases, owner control coordination and authoritative hardware snapshots remain unresolved. Existing coarse headroom and no-victim overcommit behavior remain. ComfyUI unknown submissions need reconciliation before any future exclusive lease can safely release. | <a id="h518"></a>H518 | Browse, search and export everything the assistant has generated | keep | Echivalent | Reevaluat | The unified gallery now scans retained generated and attachment metadata through bounded keyset pages. Search and kind filters apply during each scan, including MIME matches; zero-match pages retain continuation to older records. Cursor fields, ordering and filter/source binding are checked before catalog or header I/O. Each request inspects at most 200 candidate headers and retains the existing authenticated path/byte validation. The HUD resets and cancels stale searches, reports retained versus loaded/scanned counts separately, and exports an explicit selection of at most 200 files/128 MiB. Independent review and desktop/responsive-browser tests cover older-only matches, empty-page continuation, deletion boundaries, source flags, malformed cursors and exact export selection. Shared attachment previews now abort on card unmount and reject late fetch/body continuations; owned object URLs are revoked even before a pending React state update commits. Reassessment against the frozen keep/superior row confirms the bounded retained-catalog browse/search/export contract. Retention and unavailable-byte handling match the original scope; deleted/evicted history restoration, native controls and unlimited ZIPs are not acceptance requirements. Timestamp/key traversal excludes newer appends without promising exact membership against backdated inserts. The current local generator catalogs produced assets; legacy cloud proposals do not execute a cloud image backend. Future approved cloud completion must persist catalog output as part of H515, not be inferred from generic text-task completion. No live-provider or universal producer-provenance proof is claimed. The implemented cloud-image producer now writes canonical validated output and an idempotent opt-in catalog record before successful completion; independent signed-worker/mock-provider/blob/ZIP checks cover the previously conditional writeback obligation. The legacy generic text task is not treated as an image producer. The unified gallery opens and exports retained entries. Scheduled reminders now send 1..8 exact owner-bound media IDs (32 MiB aggregate) through the real Telegram binary/text adapter under one validated 1..300-second total deadline, default 300, including lookup, reads, all files, final text and held flushing. Durable sending/partial/unknown states, current recipient/content checks and process locks prevent automatic replay, stale-held duplication and late-ack continuation. Independent review confirms owner-authored IDs satisfy the frozen cron-media clause; no model MEDIA parser or every-channel requirement was accepted. At the original review: fresh 148 reader/generation/media checks, independent 98 backend/16 frontend checks and full 1,315 frontend tests pass. Synchronous local I/O is not preemptible, but no subsequent dispatch occurs after expiry. This is code equivalence with mocked HTTP; no live Telegram, new-provider/native-codec or arbitrary-path guarantee. The complete integrated backend suite collected 11,428 tests with 11,404 passes, 23 skips and one expected failure; no unexpected failures. Workdir integration rechecked previously-current evidence against base 837d6d08: additive script-only option validation and adjacent HUD fields leave the media contract unchanged. Existing media and frontend regressions remain green. [d1](../agents/core/image_generation_runtime.py) [d2](../agents/core/image_generation_view.py) [d3](../agents/core/routers/multimodal.py) |

| <a id="h529"></a>H529 | Transcribe a voice message or dictated speech | update | Parțial | Reevaluat | Un singur backend. Hermes are opt (faster-whisper local, \`local_command\`, Groq, OpenAI, Mistral Voxtral cu diarizare și timestamp-uri pe cuvânt, xAI, ElevenLabs Scribe, DeepInfra) plus provideri pe comandă de shell, un ABC \`TranscriptionProvider\`, selecție explicită strictă cu auto-detect doar la o instalare neconfigurată vreodată, un plafon pe lungimea promptului Whisper, trunchierea liniștii înainte de upload ca să scadă octeții trimiși, un picker și \`POST /api/audio/transcribe\`. Nimic din astea nu e livrat. Nu din lene: clauza de guvernanță a rândului spune că orice STT în cloud trece prin \`plugin.egress\` și trimite vorbirea înregistrată a owner-ului în afara cutiei — e aceeași clasă de dezvăluire ca TTS-ul în cloud și trebuie clasificat, nu pus ca implicit de config. Cache-ul motorului stă tot în \`routers/voice.py\`, care e casa greșită pentru el; canalul îl importă în loc să-l dubleze, iar mutarea lui e o schimbare separată. NEVERIFICAT pe servicii reale: niciun model Whisper încărcat cu adevărat. [d1](../agents/core/voice/stt.py) [d2](../agents/core/voice/hallucination.py) [d3](../agents/core/channels/inbound_voice.py) |
| <a id="h530"></a>H530 | Not turn a silent recording into a message | copy | Echivalent | Reevaluat | Whisper nu tace când nu aude nimic — inventează. Pe liniște întoarce o propoziție fluentă din datele de antrenament: generice de subtitrare („Subtitles by the Amara.org community", „Subtitrarea: ..."), formule de încheiere („Thanks for watching!", „Mulțumesc pentru vizionare!"). Pentru o casetă de dictare e o enervare; aici e un control de siguranță, exact cum spune clauza de guvernanță a rândului: un mesaj vocal devine o tură \*inbound\*, o tură inbound poate cere lucruri, deci un transcript halucinat e o instrucțiune pe care n-a autorizat-o nimeni, intrată într-un sistem guvernat. \`hallucination.py\` o oprește. Designul e dictat de asimetria erorilor: a pierde un mesaj real e inacceptabil (cineva zice „mulțumesc" și dispare fără răspuns), a lăsa unul să treacă costă o tură ignorabilă. De aici două niveluri, nu o listă: frazele distinctive (\`CANNED\`) cad pe text; cele pe care oamenii chiar le spun (\`AMBIGUOUS\`) cad doar când durata \*decodată\* arată că înregistrarea era prea scurtă ca să le conțină — iar un apelant care nu știe durata le păstrează. Potrivirea e pe transcriptul întreg, niciodată pe substring; regula pentru generice cere marker ȘI vocabular exclusiv de generic, fiindcă prima variantă (doar markerul) mânca „am văzut pe amara.org un film bun". Filtrul rulează în \`STTEngine.transcribe\` și întoarce \`\[silence\]\` — santinela care exista deja și pe care fiecare apelant o tratează ca „nu s-a spus nimic" — deci ruta de dictare din browser o primește gratuit, nu doar calea nouă de pe Telegram. CORECTARE la auditul din 07.09: verdictul „missing" era pe jumătate greșit. A doua cerință a rândului — auto-stop pe liniște (VAD), ca utilizatorul să nu apese o tastă — EXISTĂ de mult în \`frontend/src/voice.ts\`: \`recordUtterance\` eșantionează RMS la 60 ms și oprește după \`SILENCE_MS\`, cu plafon dur și abandon când nimeni nu vorbește. Auditul a dat un \`rg -i 'vad'\` peste \`frontend/src/\` și a raportat „nimic relevant"; linia 111 spune „VAD" în litere. Ce lipsea cu adevărat era dovada: nimic nu o testa, fiindcă harness-ul comun folosește un MediaRecorder care se oprește singur și scurtcircuitează intervalul. \`voice-vad.test.tsx\` folosește unul care NU se oprește niciodată singur — dacă o tură se termină acolo, doar detectorul de liniște a putut-o termina — și acoperă fereastra de liniște, plafonul, abandonul și faptul că vorbirea continuă resetează ceasul. | <a id="h545"></a>H545 | Let the editor hand the agent MCP servers that exist only for that session | copy | Lipsă | Reevaluat | Every accepted part is missing: (1) an ACP session entry point (session/new, load, resume, fork) that accepts an mcpServers array; this depends on H542. (2) A session-scoped MCP registry layered over the global MCPManager, so that a server registered for one session is invisible to every other session and is torn down when the session ends. (3) Off-loop registration (asyncio.to_thread) with a bounded discovery join (default 1.5 s), so a dead server cannot block session creation. (4) A scheduled late refresh that re-reads the session's tool list after slow discovery finishes. (5) Governance: session-supplied servers pass through the same outbound MCP authorization gate as configured servers (H554, still partial), and their trust tier is capped at no wider than a configured server's. | <a id="h551"></a>H551 | Choose which of a server's tools the model may see | copy | Echivalent | Reevaluat | Filtrele allow/deny pe nume și glob (\`tools_allow\`/\`tools_deny\`, potrivite cu \`fnmatchcase\` pe numele original al uneltei) restrâng atât listarea, cât și apelarea uneltelor MCP; deny prevalează (\`tool_visible\`, agents/core/mcp/client.py:603-610). \`_list_tools\` nu oferă o unealtă ascunsă (:596-601), iar \`MCPServer.call_tool\` răspunde \`tool_filtered\` înainte să plece vreun frame (:616-617), deci scoaterea unei unelte din filtru o revocă imediat, și la apel, nu doar la înregistrare. Un filtru malformat îngustează, nu lărgește: un allow care nu e listă devine \`\[\]\` (\`_clean_tool_patterns\`, :124-135). Limitele (64 de pattern-uri / 128 de caractere, :120-121) sunt fail-closed. Ruta de add (\`admin_mcp_add\`, agents/core/routers/mcp.py:165-174) refuză cu 400 \`{"error": "invalid_tool_filter", "field": ...}\` un \`tools_allow\` sau \`tools_deny\` peste limite, în loc să-l taie (\`tool_patterns_over_bounds\`, client.py:138-154), la fel ca pe o valoare care nu e listă sau pe o intrare goală. Un deny care nu poate fi păstrat (peste 64 de pattern-uri, un pattern peste 128 de caractere, sau care nu e listă) venit dintr-un config editat manual prin \`load_from_config\` îngustează serverul la nimic (\`tools_allow = \[\]\`) și loghează un avertisment (:407-418), pentru că un deny tăiat ar face din nou vizibilă unealta pe care o numea. Filtrele fac round-trip prin \`to_config\`/\`load_from_config\` (:808-809, :846-847). Teste (tests/test_mcp_tool_filters.py, 13/13 verzi): listare și deny prevalează (tests/test_mcp_tool_filters.py:47-57), unealtă filtrată refuzată la apel pe nume (tests/test_mcp_tool_filters.py:60-70), filtru malformat → nimic și round-trip de config (tests/test_mcp_tool_filters.py:73-84), ruta primește filtre și refuză formele malformate (tests/test_mcp_tool_filters.py:98-109), ruta refuză 129 de caractere / 65 de intrări pe ambele câmpuri (tests/test_mcp_tool_filters.py:121-129) și acceptă un filtru exact la limită (tests/test_mcp_tool_filters.py:132-138), un deny imposibil de păstrat ascunde toate uneltele cu avertisment (tests/test_mcp_tool_filters.py:141-147), iar un config editat manual cu deny peste limite se încarcă fail-closed fără să atingă serverul vecin (tests/test_mcp_tool_filters.py:150-159). Resursele/prompts sunt urmărite separat în H552. [d1](../agents/core/mcp/client.py) [d2](../agents/core/routers/mcp.py) [d3](../tests/test_mcp_tool_filters.py) |

| <a id="h557"></a>H557 | Make a third-party tool schema safe and unambiguous for every model provider | copy | Parțial | Reevaluat | No provider-agnostic repair of a third-party MCP server's JSON Schema exists (re-verified at HEAD 2026-09-22, line numbers re-mapped 2026-09-23 after the fail-closed tool-filter bounds change, which touches none of this path; #1179 changed nothing on this path either — its client.py hunks are the stdio \`cwd_secret_files\` disclosure at :17-36, :58, :211-242 and :560-571; the only change since the 2026-09-07 stamping that bears on this row is #1152 adding a fifth unrepaired provider path, below). (1) \`agents/core/mcp/client.py:584-601\` \`_list_tools\` still stores \`inputSchema\` verbatim (\`:592\`) into \`MCPTool.input_schema\` (\`:357\`); add a \`_normalize_mcp_input_schema\` applied at registration. (2) Two of Hermes' five repairs are absent on every path, verified by running the code: draft-07 \`definitions\`/\`#/definitions/...\` are never promoted to \`$defs\`/\`#/$defs/...\` (and \`gemini_schema\` silently reduces a \`$ref\` property to \`{}\`, deleting the shape), and a Pydantic nullable union \`anyOf:\[{...},{"type":"null"}\], default:null\` is never collapsed to its non-null branch (\`gemini_schema\` yields \`anyOf:\[{"type":"string"},{}\]\` with \`default:null\` retained on the Gemini path; the other four paths ship the raw nullable union untouched). (3) The three repairs that do exist (\`required\` pruned to declared names \`:424-428\`, missing \`type\` coerced \`:421\`, empty-OBJECT avoided \`:447-449\`) live only in \`agents/core/llm/tool_dialects.py:369-451\` and therefore apply to Gemini alone; the other four serialization paths pass \`input_schema\` through unrepaired: \`anthropic_tools\` (tool_dialects.py:147-151), \`ToolSpec.as_openai\` (tool_protocol.py:19-27; used by base.py:553, the Ollama path base.py:805 and openrouter.py:95), the OpenAI Responses payload (responses.py:72-81) and the xAI Responses payload (xai.py:79-80, whose \`_payload\` overrides responses.py's, added by #1152 on 2026-09-15). (4) No name sanitization or provider-legal prefixing: \`MCPTool.qualified_name\` (client.py:367-370) is \`server/tool\`, whose \`/\` is outside the \`\[A-Za-z0-9_-\]\` function-name charset both Anthropic and OpenAI enforce, and nothing replaces characters outside \`\[A-Za-z0-9_\]\`; \`grep -rn "mcp__" agents/\` returns nothing. (5) The row's security property — a third-party tool shadowing a built-in governed tool name — is unaddressed: \`_resolve\` (client.py:748-777) disambiguates only among \`self.servers\` (:768), never against the agent tool registry. (6) No test covers any of this; \`grep -rln "gemini_schema" tests/\` returns only tests/test_cloud_tool_turns.py, and neither test that constructs \`MCPTool\` (tests/test_mcp_hardening.py, tests/test_mcp_trust.py) asserts on \`input_schema\`. All of the above is currently unreachable by a model because no MCP-client -\> tool-registry bridge exists (\`MCPManager.get_tools()\`, client.py:742-746, has zero callers repo-wide), so the normalizer plus its tests should land inside whichever PR first offers MCP client tools to the model, not as a standalone pure function — and that normalizer must sit at registration, ahead of all five dialect serializers, not inside \`gemini_schema\`. | <a id="h575"></a>H575 | Read the product's own per-feature manual (published docs site) | copy | Parțial | Audit 07.09 | Reevaluare pe cod; cerințele și lipsurile sunt în rândul original.  |
| <a id="h576"></a>H576 | Read a PDF / Office document / notebook as text from inside a tool call | update | Parțial | Reevaluat | Formats stop at .pdf and .docx: no .ipynb, .xlsx, .doc, .ppt, .odt, .rtf or .epub, and the text comes back plain rather than converted to Markdown. There is no size refusal for documents (Hermes refuses over 50 MB): the whole file is parsed again on every page request, and max_bytes bounds only the text returned. There is no scanned-page coverage warning naming the page ranges that yielded no text, which the row calls the clever half. Paging is by byte offset, not a line offset/limit window. A missing parser is named (\`parser_missing\`) rather than installed lazily. [d1](../agents/core/file_tools.py) [d2](../tests/test_file_search.py) [d3](../tests/test_file_read_documents.py) |
| <a id="h577"></a>H577 | Give the current conversation one recurring instruction (session heartbeat) | copy | Parțial | Audit 07.09 | Reevaluare pe cod; cerințele și lipsurile sunt în rândul original.  |

| <a id="h579"></a>H579 | Pull a file or slice of a file into a message inline (@ context references) | copy | Lipsă | Reevaluat | The expander needs to be built. It must parse \`@file:path\` and \`@file:path#Lstart-end\` in the owner's message and resolve every path through FileScope. It must reject binary files, cap bytes per reference and in total, and append the content under \`--- Attached Context ---\`. Refused, missing or truncated references should produce inline warnings instead of failing the turn. It must call mark_turn_recall_tainted whenever content is attached, so kernel GRANT decisions escalate to QUEUE. It should run in the /chat and /chat/stream turn path, so both the HUD and \`nerva chat\` get it. Completion over reference types and in-scope paths also needs to be added: a user-guarded, read-only FileScope listing that the InputBar queries when the owner types \`@\`. [d1](../agents/core/file_tools.py) [d2](../agents/core/security/recall_taint.py) [d3](../agents/core/security/taint.py) |

| <a id="h583"></a>H583 | Steer which upstream provider serves an OpenRouter request (provider routing) | copy | Echivalent | Reevaluat | OpenRouter provider routing ships, and the owner's changes reach the live backend on the next request. Six llm rows are seeded right after compatible_provider (agents/core/settings_db.py:144-149): openrouter_sort, openrouter_only, openrouter_ignore, openrouter_order, openrouter_require_parameters, and openrouter_data_collection, seeded deny. The HUD PUT /api/admin/settings/llm and \`nerva config set\` both validate through settings_db.validate_category, and the three slug lists share one slug check (agents/core/settings_db.py:521 -\> agents/core/llm/provider_routing.py:97). build_provider_block (agents/core/llm/provider_routing.py:110) turns the knobs into OpenRouter's \`provider\` object. It lower-cases and de-duplicates slugs, caps each list at 32, sends require_parameters only as a literal true, omits empty knobs, and raises on an invalid value instead of dropping it. HybridRouter.detect hands OpenRouterBackend a reader over the rows, not a snapshot (agents/core/llm/hybrid_router.py:395). Before every text or tool-turn request the backend re-reads and re-validates them off the event loop (agents/core/llm/openrouter.py:94, called at agents/core/llm/openrouter.py:163 and agents/core/llm/openrouter.py:201) and puts the object in the body (agents/core/llm/openrouter.py:124). A HUD change, or a \`nerva config set\` run from another process against the same settings DB, therefore governs the next request, so the CLI's '(a running hub picks it up within 30 s)' holds for these rows. The object goes only to the openrouter profile, never to openai-compatible (agents/core/llm/openrouter.py:70). Fail-closed limits: a stored value that decodes but does not validate refuses to build the backend at detect (agents/core/llm/hybrid_router.py:398, logged). After detect it refuses that request: '\[OpenRouter error\]', nothing sent, and an error log naming llm.openrouter_\* (agents/core/llm/openrouter.py:107-111). The next request flows once the row is fixed. Known limit: a row that cannot be read or decoded (a sqlite error, or malformed JSON written around the API) is swallowed by settings_db.get_value into that knob's seeded default with no log line. Such a request keeps data_collection deny but loses only/ignore/order/sort/require_parameters. A 400/404 to a request that carried the object logs OpenRouter's own message (log-safe, 200 chars), the object sent and the settings to loosen (agents/core/llm/openrouter.py:128), so the seeded deny does not fail opaquely after an upgrade. Coverage is tests/test_openrouter_provider_routing.py (30 tests). It checks the exact body on the tool-turn and text paths (tests/test_openrouter_provider_routing.py:78, tests/test_openrouter_provider_routing.py:93), and on the wire through the real egress client with a MockTransport injected through its public transport kwarg (tests/test_openrouter_provider_routing.py:135). It checks that no object is sent with nothing set or for openai-compatible (tests/test_openrouter_provider_routing.py:102, tests/test_openrouter_provider_routing.py:114), and that bad values are refused (tests/test_openrouter_provider_routing.py:174, tests/test_openrouter_provider_routing.py:180). It covers the rows, the deny default, write validation and the CLI entry point (tests/test_openrouter_provider_routing.py:192, tests/test_openrouter_provider_routing.py:206, tests/test_openrouter_provider_routing.py:223), and router wiring with a stubbed read (tests/test_openrouter_provider_routing.py:274, tests/test_openrouter_provider_routing.py:287, tests/test_openrouter_provider_routing.py:300). Three router tests run on a real temporary settings DB: rows -\> detect -\> body (tests/test_openrouter_provider_routing.py:346); a change after detect reaching the next request, tightening and loosening (tests/test_openrouter_provider_routing.py:358); and a tampered row after detect refusing the request, then healing (tests/test_openrouter_provider_routing.py:384). Two more cover the policy-404 hint and its absence without an object or on a 401 (tests/test_openrouter_provider_routing.py:406, tests/test_openrouter_provider_routing.py:424). Nerva has no Nous Portal provider, so the knobs apply to OpenRouter only. [d1](../agents/core/llm/provider_routing.py) [d2](../agents/core/llm/openrouter.py) [d3](../agents/core/llm/hybrid_router.py) |

| <a id="h594"></a>H594 | Have the agent pick up a project's conventions automatically (context files) | copy | Lipsă | Reevaluat | Runtime discovery needs to be built. It must resolve the working directory (the FileScope root or the terminal cwd) and walk from its git root down to it, collecting AGENTS.md, .cursorrules and .cursor/rules/\*.mdc. It must discover again when tools move into a subdirectory, and must never load SOUL.md from a project. Every file goes through FileScope, gets a per-file and a total byte budget with explicit truncation, and is scanned with detect_injection (preferably detect_injection_normalized); a flagged file becomes a \[BLOCKED ...\] placeholder. Loading any file marks the turn tainted. The result is injected as a caveated prompt block beside the core-memory block. Tests must cover the walk, the budgets, blocking and the taint. Deliberate native-fd bypass output is not a redirected Python stream. Stream spills are capped at 5,000,000 bytes per stream: later bytes are counted but not kept, so only the inline tail shows a runaway stream's end. Hermes project\|strict mode remains intentionally represented by Nerva tool profiles (agent x surface x principal), not copied as a separate switch. | <a id="h596"></a>H596 | Keep working when one API key is rate-limited (credential pools) | update | Parțial | Reevaluat | The accepted branches are missing. _ROTATABLE_STATUS (agents/core/llm/auth_rotation.py:28) is {401, 403, 429}, so a 402 billing failure neither rotates nor gets the long (1 h) cooldown. No OAuth credential type exists, and a 401 has no refresh-then-rotate step. Keys resolve only from os.environ in from_env; VaultResolver (agents/core/secrets_vault.py:16) is never consulted for LLM keys. The owner also cannot see partial degradation. AuthProfilesPanel shows one healthy/failing tag per provider, derived from the pool-level healthy count, so a pool with some keys cooling still reads green. ## delta

| ID | Capabilitate | Decizie inițială | Stare cod | Bază | Observație / restanță |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| <a id="h656"></a>H656 | A group chat between several agents that keeps running when the client that opened it is closed | update | Parțial | Audit 07.09 | Reevaluare pe cod; cerințele și lipsurile sunt în rândul original.  |
| <a id="h657"></a>H657 | A surviving host continues a room whose owner-host died, and fences the old host if it comes back | skip | Exclus intenționat | Audit 07.09 | Preluat din auditul 07.09; nereevaluat în această livrare.  |
| <a id="h658"></a>H658 | A member of a shared session that lives on a different machine, running under a capability the target machine issued and can revoke | update | Parțial | Audit 07.09 | Reevaluare pe cod; cerințele și lipsurile sunt în rândul original.  |
| <a id="h659"></a>H659 | A retried external request returns the original run instead of starting a second one | copy | Lipsă | Reevaluat | The whole accepted row is missing: - Idempotency-Key header parsing, with 1–255 visible-ASCII validation and HTTP 400 invalid_idempotency_key. - A durable UNIQUE(scope,key) SQLite reservation taken under BEGIN IMMEDIATE that survives restarts and multiple workers. It stores only a request fingerprint and a public status/result reference, never bodies or credentials. - A caller-derived scope (webhook id, A2A peer, principal) so two callers cannot collide. - A duplicate returns the original action/run, and a key reused with a different fingerprint is refused. - A \`durable\` property on the store. - Placement in front of the kernel/approval enqueue, so a retry never becomes a second approval item or audit row. It has to be wired into /api/actions/request, /api/webhooks/{id} and /api/a2a/task, and into the run-create route once H476 lands. [d1](../agents/core/routers/actions.py) [d2](../agents/core/autonomy/action_approvals.py) [d3](../agents/core/routers/webhooks.py) |
| <a id="h660"></a>H660 | Code the agent runs keeps its variables, imports and loaded data between calls | copy | Parțial | Reevaluat | Generic SSH, Modal and remote TLS provisioning remain unsupported and require an explicitly isolated governed transport design. Docker selection currently supports local Unix endpoints. Correlation tokens/nonces reject stale frames but cannot attest output against Python introspection inside the same worker; authorization is enforced by the host broker. Spills cover redirected Python stdout/stderr up to 5,000,000 bytes per stream; deliberate native-fd bypasses and the part of a runaway stream past that ceiling are not kept on disk. Production deployment evidence is separate from these real-container tests. [d1](../agents/core/tool_rpc_runtime.py) [d2](../agents/core/memory/session_search.py) [d3](../agents/core/autonomy_coordinator.py) |
| <a id="h661"></a>H661 | Oversized tool output is paged from a file instead of being thrown away | update | Echivalent | Reevaluat | Oversized output is paged back from its spill file instead of being re-run. execute_code stdout/stderr are spooled to disk as they arrive (agents/core/code_tools.py:468, agents/core/tool_result_store.py:428) under \<data root\>/workspace/tool_results. Each stream is capped at DEFAULT_MAX_STREAM_BYTES = 5,000,000 (agents/core/tool_result_store.py:127): StreamSpill.write stops writing at the cap but keeps counting (agents/core/tool_result_store.py:597), close appends one '\[... spill capped at N bytes: M more bytes ...\]' marker (agents/core/tool_result_store.py:639), and the result carries \<stream\>_spill_capped and \<stream\>_kept_bytes (agents/core/code_tools.py:273-274). Head and tail stay inline with omitted-byte accounting (agents/core/environments/output_limits.py:42, tests/test_environment_output_limits.py:38). A whole result the loop spills (agents/core/agent_runtime.py:1315) keeps both ends in its preview (agents/core/tool_result_store.py:702-703, tests/test_tool_loop_result_spill.py:139), and file_read itself is never spilled (tests/test_tool_loop_result_spill.py:287). Every notice carries the exact call from page_recipe (agents/core/tool_result_store.py:142): file_read(path="\<resolved path\>", offset=0, max_bytes=N). The same arguments are returned as \<stream\>_read_with (agents/core/code_tools.py:271) and read_with (agents/core/tool_result_store.py:733), and stream recipes propose pages of at most 8,000 bytes (agents/core/code_tools.py:225). file_read takes an integer offset from 0 to MAX_OFFSET = 2\*\*53-1 (agents/core/file_tools.py:122, agents/core/file_tools.py:1476). Anything else is refused as bad_offset, both in the preflight and in a direct call (agents/core/file_tools.py:1395, agents/core/file_tools.py:728). An offset at or past the end is an empty final page, answered without seeking (agents/core/file_tools.py:746). A page that stops early returns next_offset (agents/core/file_tools.py:1250) and never ends inside a UTF-8 character (agents/core/file_tools.py:1202). A .pdf/.docx pages through its extracted text (agents/core/file_tools.py:1254). The named call is one file_read answers in every live setup, including the three the review found broken. The recipe path is resolved (agents/core/tool_result_store.py:204). A spill is never named like a secret (agents/core/tool_result_store.py:187). file_read alone gets a read-only door for exact spill-file names in the coordinator's spill directory (agents/core/file_tools.py:636, agents/core/file_tools.py:673, agents/core/file_tools.py:1167), wired at agents/core/autonomy_coordinator.py:796. So JARVIS_FILE_ROOTS pointing elsewhere, a symlinked JARVIS_HOME or a bridged tool with a secret-looking name no longer strands the file (tests/test_spill_paging.py:507, tests/test_spill_paging.py:535); the door's limits are pinned by tests/test_spill_paging.py:414. The recipe is named only where that call is answered. ToolResultStore.readable(path) (agents/core/tool_result_store.py:358) asks the coordinator probe (agents/core/autonomy_coordinator.py:800). The probe requires four things: file_read registered, allowed by the job toolset, in this turn's profile offer (noted at agents/core/autonomy_coordinator.py:896), and able to open this very path (agents/core/autonomy_coordinator.py:812, agents/core/file_tools.py:701). execute_code also requires file_read in its own run's offer (agents/core/code_tools.py:452, agents/core/code_tools.py:547). Otherwise the notice says the file is kept for the owner and cannot be paged from this turn (tests/test_spill_paging.py:545, tests/test_spill_paging.py:589, tests/test_code_tools.py:866), and concurrent turns each see their own offer (tests/test_spill_paging.py:627). A failed spill just omits the path (agents/core/code_tools.py:257-258, tests/test_code_tools.py:698). Retention is unchanged (agents/core/tool_result_store.py:487, tests/test_tool_result_store.py:214, tests/test_tool_result_store.py:353). End to end, a real AgentToolRuntime follows read_with and then next_offset through the real file_read and rebuilds the spilled result byte for byte, while the producing tool runs once (tests/test_spill_paging.py:719). execute_code stdout does the same on the one-shot and session paths (tests/test_code_tools.py:824), and a runaway stream stops at its ceiling with one marker (tests/test_code_tools.py:877, tests/test_spill_paging.py:233). Adaptations: offsets are bytes, not Hermes' lines. The 5,000,000-byte cap covers streamed spills, which is Hermes' execute_code scope; whole-result JSON spills stay bounded by the 256 MB retention sweep. A spill stays third-party when it comes back: every file directly in a spill directory counts as one (agents/core/file_tools.py:687), a file_read page of it and a file_search whose matches reach it declare tainted (agents/core/file_tools.py:753, agents/core/file_tools.py:905), and the loop fences the result as DATA and raises the turn's recall taint, in the turn that spilled it or any later one (tests/test_spill_paging.py:761, tests/test_spill_paging.py:812, tests/test_spill_paging.py:834, tests/test_spill_paging.py:856). A trusted tool's spill counts too, because a spill's file name cannot say reliably which tool wrote it. [d1](../agents/core/file_tools.py) [d2](../agents/core/tool_result_store.py) [d3](../agents/core/code_tools.py) |
| <a id="h662"></a>H662 | A second local browser engine, and browsing with a snapshot of the owner's real signed-in profile | skip | Exclus intenționat | Audit 07.09 | Preluat din auditul 07.09; nereevaluat în această livrare.  |
| <a id="h663"></a>H663 | The product points at its own features — an anchored bubble while idle, and the agent raising the same bubble mid-conversation | copy | Parțial | Audit 07.09 | Reevaluare pe cod; cerințele și lipsurile sunt în rândul original.  |

| <a id="h671"></a>H671 | A long-lived conversation knows what day it is now | copy | Echivalent | Reevaluat | Durable session birth is resolved through an immutable, generation-checked continuation root. Explicit owner-created child sessions carry bounded retained context and root birth through multiple generations, text/stream/process entrypoints and restart without switching the shared default. SQLite atomically commits child identity, seed, lineage and initial clock; idempotent request receipts resolve retries. Corrupt newer snapshots, reused identities and failed recovery stop dispatch or writes instead of silently replacing context. Accepted history/tool compaction alone advances the durable rebuild clock through compare-and-swap; request snapshots preserve same-day prefix bytes, reject late inherited commits, and add the trusted current-date line only when rebuild and root-birth days differ. Production rendering resolves explicit TZ or existing local IANA discovery and uses historical/current DST rules with an explicit UTC offset. Unknown birth is omitted; invalid or unavailable zone discovery preserves truthful offset-only rendering rather than inventing an IANA name. Independent actual-route, Agent, restart, concurrency, cancellation, failed-resume and observe-only regressions support the full frozen clock contract. This is code-equivalence, not a live-provider claim; ordinary subsequent JSON persistence remains best effort and turn leases are process-local. H672 hangs the persona re-read on the same commit: \`_history_for_prompt\`'s \`publish()\` calls \`_refresh_souls_at_boundary\` after the clock CAS and only when the compressor reported a compression, so a refused \`commit_clock\` migrates nothing and an accepted one is followed by the refresh in that order (\`test_a_refused_clock_commit_migrates_nothing\`, \`test_an_accepted_clock_commit_is_followed_by_the_refresh_in_that_order\`); the re-read neither reads nor writes the clock, so the same-day prefix bytes and the current-date line are untouched by it. #1188's edit to \`orchestrator.py\` is the pairing store's registry path, nothing on this row. [d1](../agents/core/agent.py) [d2](../agents/core/checkpoint.py) [d3](../agents/core/context_compressor.py) |
| <a id="h672"></a>H672 | A never-ending session picks up config, skill and tool changes at the compaction boundary | copy | Parțial | Reevaluat | \*\*The boundaries are opt-in, so on a shipped default nothing migrates\*\*: the conversation-level commit exists only with \`memory.context_compression\` on (default off) and the in-turn fold only with \`llm.tool_loop_enabled\` on (default off) — a default install still needs a restart for a persona edit, exactly as before. \*\*Profile revocations are not enforced on the execution path\*\*: \`ToolRPCServer.handle\` checks the job toolset, registration and the gate, never the tool profile, and the executor's gate map is rebuilt only at a fold — so in a loop that never folds (small results, the common shape) a tool the profile withdrew mid-turn stays offered \*and\* executes until the loop ends; pre-existing, unchanged by this slice, pinned as a limit by \`test_between_folds_a_profile_revocation_is_not_enforced_by_the_executor_either\`. \*\*The persona migration is process-wide\*\*: \`orchestrator.agents\` is every agent and \`agent.soul\` is the one dict every session's system prompt reads, so the session that commits migrates the persona for all sessions (the boundary log line says so); refreshing only the routed agents would need the route at \`_history_for_prompt\`, which it does not have. \*\*On the staged shared-route path the turn's budget is computed against the persona in force before the commit\*\* (\`_shared_route_plan\` → \`build()\` measures \`agent.soul\` while planning; the refresh runs in \`accept()\` → \`publish()\`), so a persona that grew is sent that turn with a budget computed for the old size — bounded by \`_soul_max_chars\`, self-correcting next turn; the H363 cached prefix and the H673 usage anchor under-count for one turn after a rebuild the same way. The probe trusts the kernel's signature; what the builder reads outside the file (the scan patterns) is code and needs a restart. The prompt half covers the SOUL only: Nerva's plugin and skills sections live in the user prompt and were already per-turn, so there is no plugin section in the system prompt to fail open — the fail-open is exercised on the SOUL builder. The in-turn boundary does not rebuild \`messages\[0\]\`: a persona change reaches the \*next\* turn, not the loop in flight. "A newly configured MCP server appears without a restart" is only as true as the MCP manager's registration into ToolRPC is hot, which this slice did not verify. The lineage row stays at \`nerva.context-compaction.v1\`; the boundary note is a log line plus the runtime event, not a field on the row. \`refresh_tools\` compares names, so a tool whose schema changed under the same name is adopted only because the fresh list is taken whenever the set moved, never on its own. The stale comment at \`agents/core/file_tools.py\` saying \`_load_soul\` runs no injection scan predates H387 and is left for a hygiene commit (it would drift five unrelated rows). Nine ledger rows cite \`agent.py\`, \`agent_runtime.py\` or \`orchestrator.py\` and were drifted by this branch (and by #1188 on main: its registry path in \`orchestrator.py\`, and the pairing kwarg, refusal branch and lifespan hand-over in \`telegram.py\` and \`web.py\`, which H146, H449, H364 and H671 also cite and were read against — the pairing store is not their subject); each was re-read against the diff on its cited paths before its hashes moved. Six keep their text and take fresh stamps — H146, H298, H363, H364, H449, H679: the in-turn re-resolution runs through \`_profiled\`, which still applies the job toolset on both sides of the profile, and the result-spill, cache, reasoning and job-pin mechanism those rows cite is untouched. Three are rewritten — H387: the ERROR line now comes from \`_announce_soul_verdict\` behind the \`_read_soul\` builder, for boot and boundary alike; H671 and H673: \`publish()\` re-reads the personas after the clock CAS, and the staged-route budget limit above is now on H673's own row. The 16 other needs_review rows on main (H060, H061, H068, H071, H104, H108, H130, H135, H139, H398, H477, H510, H523, H683 from #1188's edits to \`orchestrator.py\`, \`web.py\` and \`telegram.py\`; H288 and H684 from #1187's \`heartbeat.py\` and \`docs/ARCHITECTURE.md\`) are main's own drift, not this branch's, and are left untouched here; #1187's five-line comment in \`orchestrator.py\` re-drifted the nine rows above once more, and they were re-read against it and re-stamped. [d1](../agents/core/session_refresh.py) [d2](../agents/core/agent.py) [d3](../agents/core/agent_runtime.py) |

| <a id="h674"></a>H674 | Compression cannot stall the user: bounded holds, inactivity deadlines and derived thresholds | update | Parțial | Reevaluat | (1) Bounded hold: an arriving turn waits at most a configurable memory.compression_max_turn_hold_seconds (default about 10s) for an LLM summary. On expiry it proceeds with the deterministic digest, or verbatim turns when within the window, and shows a visible notice that the summary was deferred. The summary then finishes in the background and seeds _ctx_summary_cache for the next turn, without publishing through the compaction-clock CAS for the turn that did not use it. (2) Inactivity deadline: the summarizer streams and falls back to the digest after N seconds with no tokens (default 60) instead of holding the full read budget; a slow-but-alive stream is not killed. (3) Tests: a hanging summarizer returns within the hold; a trickling summarizer survives; a silent one is cut at the inactivity deadline. [d1](../agents/core/context_compressor.py) [d2](../agents/core/orchestrator.py) [d3](../agents/core/llm/base.py) |

(2) Slack and Discord do not stream: both descriptors declare supports_edit=True (agents/core/channels/slack.py:102, agents/core/channels/discord.py:32) but neither adapter implements begin_stream or an edit call (Slack's send posts via chat_postMessage only, slack.py:329), and _begin_channel_draft refuses both because a streamed draft would publish tokens before the channel.reply approval; streaming there needs an approval contract that covers output not yet generated. (3) No acknowledgement at ingest: Telegram sends a typing chat action only while reading an inbound attachment (telegram.py:595) and a record_voice action before a spoken reply, so an ordinary text message gets no receipt signal until the first streamed token or the whole reply; Slack and Discord send none (the Slack Socket Mode envelope ack is a transport ack to Slack, not a signal the sender sees).
