| <a id="h691"></a>H691 | Reverse-proxy trust is a validated, bounded allowlist rather than an on/off switch | update | Echivalent | Reevaluat | JARVIS_TRUSTED_PROXIES is the only allowlist that decides whose forwarding headers count: X-Forwarded-For / X-Real-IP and X-Forwarded-Proto alike. Parsing and refusals: parse_trusted_proxies (agents/core/proxy_trust.py:134) reads each entry with ip_network(strict=False) (agents/core/proxy_trust.py:160), so a bare address becomes its own /32 or /128. It refuses '\*', 0.0.0.0/0 and ::/0 by name (agents/core/proxy_trust.py:107), any prefix length 0 (agents/core/proxy_trust.py:165), unreadable entries and more than 64 entries, naming the variable and the position, never the value. A malformed list refuses boot through assert_parseable_trusted_proxies (agents/core/proxy_trust.py:411), whose message tells the operator to list the proxy's own address or a bounded CIDR range with no wildcard; it runs in enforce_boot_posture (agents/core/boot_guards.py:355) and again after .env loads in assert_front_door (agents/core/boot_guards.py:323). That is stricter than Hermes, which drops a bad entry and carries on. At request time a malformed list fails closed and is said once (agents/web.py:129). The default trusts nothing, not even loopback; the legacy JARVIS_TRUSTED_PROXY flag means loopback only (agents/core/proxy_trust.py:206). One trust set: uvicorn's own proxy-header layer used to rewrite the client address and scheme from loopback, or from whatever FORWARDED_ALLOW_IPS named, before the app looked. serve.server_config now builds uvicorn with proxy_headers=False (serve.py:84) and records that choice (serve.py:86). docker-compose.yml passes --no-proxy-headers (docker-compose.yml:13); the Dockerfile, the systemd unit, start.sh, START.bat and the packaged build already start through serve.py. The app reads X-Forwarded-Proto itself, only from a listed peer and only when there is exactly one http/https/ws/wss value (forwarded_proto, agents/core/proxy_trust.py:389, applied by _ForwardedProtoMiddleware, agents/web.py:163, registered at agents/web.py:595), so a TLS proxy still yields https:// base URLs for the MCP resource and the widget snippet. The scheme is set on the scope in place, as uvicorn's layer did, so the route the router records reaches the golden-signals middleware and a forwarded request keeps its per-route metrics label (agents/web.py:194, tests/test_trusted_proxies.py:582). assert_server_proxy_layer_within_trust (agents/core/proxy_trust.py:570) runs in both boot passes (agents/core/boot_guards.py:358, agents/core/boot_guards.py:324). It refuses FORWARDED_ALLOW_IPS, UVICORN_FORWARDED_ALLOW_IPS, or --forwarded-allow-ips on a uvicorn command line when an entry is '\*', a /0, not an address, or a peer outside JARVIS_TRUSTED_PROXIES plus uvicorn's loopback default. Gates: the localhost auth bypass (_real_client_host, agents/web.py:139) and the HF-2 rate-limit key (_client_ip, agents/web.py:317) resolve the client through forwarded_client, which trusts only a listed peer and walks the chain right to left. The HF-2 localhost exemption now follows the localhost gate's origin (agents/web.py:695), so an unlisted same-box proxy, or a listed one whose chain vouches for nothing, is not waved through. Logging: web.py's import-time read is silent (agents/web.py:121). The lifespan calls announce_trusted_proxies after setup_logging and the .env load (agents/web.py:463). When anything is trusted it logs one INFO line naming the source and the canonical networks (_log_trust_set, agents/core/proxy_trust.py:253), together with the wide-entry and legacy warnings that belong with it, which are repeated when the set was resolved before logging existed (agents/core/proxy_trust.py:294). Nothing is logged for the default. It also names uvicorn's layer whenever that layer believes anyone (agents/core/proxy_trust.py:528): on a raw uvicorn start without --no-proxy-headers, or under a launcher it cannot identify. Tests: tests/test_trusted_proxies.py covers parsing and refusal (tests/test_trusted_proxies.py:58, tests/test_trusted_proxies.py:74), the LAN-peer loopback spoof (tests/test_trusted_proxies.py:183), the INFO line (tests/test_trusted_proxies.py:295), the lifespan wiring run through the real lifespan (tests/test_trusted_proxies.py:434), the silent import (tests/test_trusted_proxies.py:416), and the served config with uvicorn's ProxyHeadersMiddleware in front, where a LAN peer sends X-Forwarded-For 127.0.0.1 under FORWARDED_ALLOW_IPS='\*' (tests/test_trusted_proxies.py:506). It also covers the throttle exemption (tests/test_trusted_proxies.py:534), X-Forwarded-Proto believed only from a listed peer (tests/test_trusted_proxies.py:562), and the uvicorn-allowlist refusals (tests/test_trusted_proxies.py:624). tests/test_o26_f6_boot_guards.py pins the new guard in both boot passes (tests/test_o26_f6_boot_guards.py:148, tests/test_o26_f6_boot_guards.py:200). tests/test_rate_limit_hf2.py and tests/test_admin_guard_hf7.py cover the two gates. Operator documentation is in docs/FLAGS.md. Known limits, which the row does not require: a raw python -m uvicorn agents.web:app without --no-proxy-headers (the CI openapi, soak and smoke jobs, and dev use) still lets uvicorn believe loopback's forwarding headers, which is Hermes's own default. On that raw start uvicorn's loopback walk can also rewrite a loopback peer into a listed proxy's address, after which Nerva's walk trusts the rest of the chain; using it needs the raw start, a list naming a non-loopback proxy and a same-box unlisted proxy that passes the client's X-Forwarded-For through verbatim. The guard caps that layer at the Nerva list plus loopback, and the boot names it at INFO. A launcher that embeds uvicorn some other way passes its allowlist out of the app's sight. The RFC 7239 Forwarded header is not parsed. Behaviour change: a same-box proxy that is not listed no longer gets per-client throttle keys or https:// base URLs through uvicorn's implicit loopback trust until it is listed (127.0.0.1/32). Re-pin to upstream's current release (v2026.8.31 or later). Read the bytes, record sha256, execute nothing, and keep the fail-before-network posture. 2. Pin format. The pin must accept the optional-skills/ root, which agents/core/skills/importer.py:113 currently rejects. Each entry must digest-verify and import the skill's reference files as well as SKILL.md, because the consolidated github skill and the pdf and AgentMail skills keep their per-workflow detail in references. 3. Catalog content. The re-pinned catalog must expose the consolidated github skill and grill-me, and drop the merged/deleted skills. 4. Move importer constants, tests and the doc-consistency check to the new tag.
